## Session — 2026-09-26 — **the lockup the right way up (QUADEMIA small over the product) and the signed-in phone bar cut to hamburger · wordmark · avatar with an unread dot on the hamburger — DS21's two open lines closed** — Claude

Two feature commits on `claude/work-session-80fe51` (`69ede99` the

then this record. The dev server ran from the first action (the
worktree's `.next` from 2026-09-23 deleted first; `/login` answered 200
after a 13 s first compile).

### Start of session

`main` and `origin/main` both at the 2026-09-24 docs commit; the remote
carries `main` and `production` only. Still unmerged and left alone:
`claude/work-session-ec68a3` (one BUILD_LIST line, "one link style").
Everything the 24th left open was offered back — the phone bar, the
NMC Prep report's home, the order of 03 Q10–Q13, the "D4 on the dev
site" tick, the prod region — and Sam took the phone bar.

### The phone bar: what real designs do (option 1)

Sam's instinct was that neither the envelope, the bell nor the avatar
belongs on a phone bar — "probably just the hamburger". Offered in
three parts: the standard phone app bar is three slots (a navigation
control, a title or wordmark, one or two actions, almost always the
avatar), a hamburger-only bar is the marketing-site pattern we already
used on the public bar, and MyNclex hides its desktop bar below 768px
for a phone bar of hamburger · wordmark · avatar with no bell and no
envelope. Our drawer already holds Messages with its count and
Announcements as rows, so both icons were duplicates. Three options:
(1) hamburger · wordmark · avatar with an unread dot on the hamburger
(recommended, MyNclex's shape); (2) hamburger and wordmark only, the
account moved into the drawer (legacy's sidebar ended with a My Account
block, so there is precedent); (3) the bell off only, the 24th's
recommendation. Sam took 1, then asked whether the dot belongs on the
avatar: no — the dot sits on the control whose tap leads to the new
thing, the avatar's menu holds nothing new, and Google's pattern makes
a dot on an avatar mean an account alert. Hamburger.

### The lockup: the mark is accepted, its presentation was not explored

Sam: does the wordmark have to carry the Q, and "the Quademia logo idea
has not really been explored well". Checked before answering:

- **MyNclex's bars are text only** — "MyNclex-RN" with the suffix in
  the accent colour, on desktop and phone. Its painted Q is the tab and
  home-screen icon only, and it was ruled there on 2026-08-22 that
  MyNclex gets no mark of its own (the tab-shows-Q / bar-shows-M
  mismatch accepted). So DS21's "MyNclex's lockup" of the 23rd was half
  true, and ours was the first bar in either product to carry the Q.
- **The logo's record is thin:** the QAcademy image in legacy's nav at
  32px and as the tab icon; a vector Q in an artifact on 2026-08-12,
  not adopted (used by mistake on 09-22, taken back); the painted Q
  supplied by Sam on 2026-08-22 and installed as icons, with MyNclex's
  own notes that it is a 997×1051 ellipse cut from AI art, that the tick
  and tassel vanish at 16px, and that a crisp small size "would need a
  simplified variant, which is artwork, not a resize"; a
  `claude/logo-design-ideas` branch named in MyNclex's identity doc
  that is not on the remote and left nothing. Offered a proper
  exploration (a brief, four to six vector directions at 16 / 28 / 180
  px, one colour, on white and navy, beside the painted Q).
- **Sam clarified:** the mark itself is accepted. The question is
  presentation — UWorld's "logo UWORLD | Nursing / | Medicine". Checked
  UWorld: the nursing site's header is one logo, the parent wordmark
  heavy with "Nursing" as a lighter descriptor; the parent's front page
  shows UWorld alone with divisions as menu categories.

The record showed the order had been settled twice by one sentence each
— DS5 brand-first on 09-22 ("the order says whose product it is, the
weight says which one you are in"), DS21 product-first on 09-23 — and
never seen side by side. So a **proof sheet**: six lockups on the real
bar chrome (option 1's hamburger · lockup · avatar at 360px, and the
public bar at desktop) with the real 64px mark, in Inter, each width
measured by the browser. A scratchpad HTML served by a dozen lines of
Node on `localhost:8766` — the pane cannot open `file://` and nothing
may be left in `public/`. The numbers are in `10-design-system.md`
DS21; the short of it: A (as built) and B (QUADEMIA small over the
product) both 166px with 76px of room at 360; C, UWorld's one line with
our coined name, 266px and overflowing by 24; F, the same line with a
division word "NMC Licensure", 249px and still over by 7 — and a
rename. Recommended B: UWorld's parent-first family idea in the shape a
phone holds, Sam's own rule of the 22nd, the same width as today, a

### What was built

- **`69ede99`** — `wordmark.tsx` swaps the lines (typed "Quademia", set
  in capitals by the style so a screen reader says the word);
  `components.css` gives the small line 10px / 700 / 0.1em tracking /

Sam signed the pane in (as himself — the tab landed on
`/admin/dashboard`, and `/student/dashboard` also rendered): at 360,

### What went wrong, and what it taught

- **The sheet's first measurement lied.** Options C and F reported the
  same width because the wordmark's flex boxes have `min-width: 0` and
  shrank to the room available while the nowrap text spilled out.
  Measure the text's `scrollWidth`, not the box. In AGENTS.md.
- **The record named another repo's pattern without looking at it.**
  "MyNclex's lockup" was borrowed on the 23rd from memory; MyNclex's
  bar is text only. Open the other repo's component before borrowing
  its name for a decision.
- The pane's screenshot timed out once while the desktop app was
  behind another window; a retry worked. `cd` into the MyNclex checkout
  moves the tool's working directory for the next call — use absolute
  paths and `git -C` instead.

### What is open

- The signed-in drawer opening from the right (DS21, Sam: later).
- The auth pages' own lockup — take the shared wordmark or not (a ⬜

- MyNclex's bar to take the same lockup so the family reads as one

### The NMC Prep walk (09)

Sam's plan from 2026-09-20: he signs in, we walk the features the
saved pages could not show. **The desktop app's pane refused the
site's own scripts, styles and images** (`ERR_BLOCKED_BY_CLIENT` on
every `/_next/static` file, the HTML alone arriving) — so Claude in
Chrome, installed and signed in by Sam in this session (his default
browser is Edge; Chrome opened from `Program Files (x86)`). Sam signed
in to NMC Prep himself, said he had paid for Premium, and allowed any
test to be taken. Sat on his account: a 20-question practice (20/20),
the day's Daily Challenge (5/5), one mock (three answered, submitted
early, 3%), and during the teardown below a second practice that
recorded **0/20** (the one answer did not register before Finish).
Notifications were left unopened.

Recorded in `09-free-account-and-gamification.md` §2: the offer, the
challenge (one set for everyone — all midwifery on an RGN account), the
leaderboard (all time, top 100, real names, per track, no own rank
below the list), **"past papers" settled as question books** (25
volumes of a question book, two from another site, one a university's
papers), the runner, the mock and its results page (their best
screen), AI Study, the community; other students' names left out.
**Measured afterwards from the leaderboard payload (totals only):** 32
of the top 100 never played the challenge, the median player about two
days, 94% of all points from practice and mocks, 73 of the 100 active
in the last week — the challenge is a light extra, not the engine,
which supports G1 before G2. **Corrected the same day:** the leaderboard
"Streak" column is streak points, not days ("70 days" was written and
withdrawn).

### Attempt reports (03 Q10–Q13)

Sam: plans made before MyNclex may never have been built here, and
MyNclex "really breaks down attempts". Two readers (MyNclex's code; this
repo's docs, `legacy/` and code), the key claims re-checked by hand.
Found: after an attempt a student gets a score, a grade word and the
review — **no breakdown by subject, topic or difficulty, for student or
admin, and nothing over time**; the portal guide and the landing page
promise "identify topics you repeatedly miss" and "monitor improvement
over time", and legacy carries the same words, so the live site makes
the promise today; `attempt_items` already carries subject, topic,
subtopic, difficulty, type and marks, so a breakdown is **queries
only**; `time_spent_s` exists and is **never written** (the runner
never measures it; legacy never did). Written into
`03-quiz-system.md` as *Attempt reports and progress*, with MyNclex and
NMC Prep for reference and four candidates, none ruled: **Q10** the
attempt report, **Q11** time per question, **Q12** progress across
attempts, **Q13** readiness and the cohort (with Q3). An answer-change
history was not proposed (a storage change).

### The teardown (from the outside, passive)

Sam asked for the architecture and engineering behind NMC Prep. Only
what a visitor's browser receives plus what his signed-in browser got
in normal use; nothing probed, no key used. Main-session scouting in
Chrome (the `/api/*` calls each view makes and their shapes, headers,
DNS, the question and grading flow), then a workflow: six analysts each
checked claim by claim by a verifier, a report, a critic (14 agents);
the "ours" column re-checked by hand. **Theirs:** Next.js 16.3.5 on
Vercel — London edge, every dynamic page and `/api` call computed in
Washington DC (iad1); Clerk for login and the paid flag; Paystack's
hosted checkout; MongoDB (the ids are ObjectIds); Cloudinary; Vercel
analytics; the signed-in product one client component on "/"; practice
downloads a course's bank without answers and the server marks at
submit, but **the Daily Challenge's answers reach the browser before it
is opened and the per-tap check never locks**; the mock clock is the
browser's; the leaderboard sends up to 100 rows of other students' ids,
names and last-active times; admin-only controls ship in the student
bundle behind a browser-side check; the CSP is report-only with nowhere
to report; no privacy policy; no error monitoring. **Ours, found on
the way:** the dev site sends **no security headers at all** (no HSTS,
framing, nosniff, referrer policy or CSP — reaches no one until
cutover); the dev database is in Ireland (`eu-west-1`), prod's region
not visible from here; no analytics, no error monitoring, no install
manifest; grading matches by option letter, not wording. The report
lives in this session's scratchpad only; **where it goes in the repo
was asked and not answered** (offered: `docs/product-plan/
reference-nmcprep.md`, doc 09's "the real competitor" softened).

### What was said, not ruled

- **Sam: NMC Prep is not a competitor** ("lol") — reference, not
  rival. My view given: not on quality; worth watching only as the free
  option students could drift to.
- **One address for the whole app** (their design) against an address
  per page (ours): ours kept as the better base for these students —
  refresh, sharing on WhatsApp, the back button, mobile data — with the
  speed gap closed by the queued `next/link` and loading-state items.
  Noted to Sam as defending what is built.
- **Clerk:** a good product, not for us now — our RLS reads Supabase's
  `auth.uid()`, sign-in is built and hardened (S9, S10), one Quademia
  account rests on the shared login table, Clerk's billing is Stripe
  only. Borrow its ideas instead: Turnstile on register, the queued
  session and login-history pages, two-step login later. Clerk pricing
  read 2026-09-24: free to 50,000 monthly retained users, Pro $25 a
  month for no badge and MFA. Advice, not a ruling.

### What went wrong, and what it taught

- **The pane cannot walk every site.** Some sites' own files are
  refused (`ERR_BLOCKED_BY_CLIENT`); the fix was Claude in Chrome. A
  sign-in made in Edge or another Chrome profile does not reach the
  extension's Chrome — check the tab is signed in before walking.
- **"Streak 70" was read as days.** A column label is not a unit;
  checked against the payload's field names after the fact.
- **A click by script did not register** and left a 0/20 practice on
  Sam's account. Confirm a selection took before finishing a sitting on
  someone else's account.
- The extension blocks tool output that looks like cookies or query
  strings; response headers had to be read by curl instead.

### What is open

- DS21's phone bar (Sam's decision; on the dev site now).
- Where the NMC Prep report lives, and whether our lessons become
  BUILD_LIST lines (security headers + an enforced CSP, error
  monitoring, usage analytics, home-screen install, a privacy policy
  under Ghana's Act 843) — asked, not answered. The security-header
  finding gets a ⬜ line now (found and not done).
- The order of 03 Q10–Q13 among everything else.
- `claude/work-session-ec68a3`'s line; the "D4 on the dev site" tick.
- The prod Supabase project's region (the Supabase tool here lists only
  the two dev projects).

`npm run lint:check`: 0 known errors, 0 warnings, unchanged.

## Session — 2026-09-23, second — **the Paystack webhook (D4), with one subscription per payment (§8 S15) under it; proven on the dev site with Paystack's own call and the tab closed; then one wordmark on both bars and a public phone menu (DS21)** — Claude

Written mid-session at Sam's request, then finished at the session's
end (the DS21 part and *At the end* below). The first two feature
commits, 11 files, +284 / −43, were **merged to `main` and deployed to
the dev site on Sam's yes** (`ba2c68c`); DS21 came after. One migration
(`20260923140000_subscriptions_one_per_payment.sql`), applied on dev.

### Start of session

The worktree's `.next` dated from 2026-09-11 — deleted before starting
the server (the AGENTS.md workaround). One unmerged branch found and
left alone: `claude/work-session-ec68a3`, in another worktree, one
commit adding a BUILD_LIST line ("One link style — a link inside a
sentence always underlined… (Sam, later)"). **Still unmerged; Sam to
say whether that line is kept.**

### The decision before the build: one Paystack business per product

Sam asked what a webhook is, and then what happens for the other apps,
since Paystack allows **one webhook address per business, per mode**.
Offered three ways: (A) a Paystack business per product under one
login, (B) one shared address that forwards by a product tag, (C) first
come first served. Recommended A: no code, nothing shared between
products (the extraction test), and it matches how the products are
already separated; B is a program belonging to no product, which the
rules forbid. Then read MyNclex's `company-registration.md` (settled
2026-09-02), which answers most of it:

- The existing Paystack business is the **Blogger + Apps Script era's**
  — the same product lineage, so it is MyNMCLicensure's. Its webhook
  field probably still pointed at alpha's Apps Script
  (`qacademy-alpha/Appscripts/Payments_App-DEV/code` handles
  `x-paystack-signature`) — I had said "the slot is free" from gamma
  and MyNclex alone and corrected it.
- It is a **Starter account at its lifetime ceiling**; live revenue
  waits on registering **Quademia Limited** (Ghana) and upgrading the
  account in place to Registered Business. So the live webhook cannot
  matter before that; the test side works.
- MyNclex's main rail is **Stripe via a UK company**, so the collision
  is only MyTeacher and a possible MyNclex Ghana button, later.
- Paystack's "Add a Business" reuses the same registration documents:
  **one company, one login, a Paystack business per product that sells
  in Ghana**, each with its own keys, webhook, transactions and
  settings. Sam's own summary of it was checked back and correct; the
  one wording fix was "same login", not "same account".
- The account still shows **QAcademy** to buyers; Sam: "we can change
  it". Suggested "MyNMCLicensure by Quademia" for the name buyers see,
  **QUADEMIA** for a short statement name if Paystack offers one (the
  plan notes ~22 characters). Nothing decided for the other apps.
- Open with Paystack when a second business is added: can two
  businesses settle into one account.

### What was built, in order

**§8 S15 One subscription per Paystack payment** (Sam ticked it
as drafted). `activatePaymentForUser()` checked for a subscription with
the reference and then inserted one, with no constraint behind it — two
callers in the same second both insert, and the buyer gets two years
for one price. Latent with two tabs; routine once a webhook races the
returning browser. A partial unique index on `subscriptions
(source_ref) where source = 'PAYSTACK'` (ADMIN writes `admin_grant`
every time and the trial the user id, so those sources are left out);
the migration refuses to run over an existing duplicate; activation
reads the unique refusal (23505) as "the other door won" and adopts
that receipt. **Rule 9 with it:** `subscriptions` still held `grant
all` for both browser roles; now `authenticated` SELECT only, `anon`
nothing, the admin page's four writes on the service role behind
`requireAdmin()`, `subscriptions_insert` / `_update` dropped. Checked:
no function or view writes the table; a rolled-back duplicate insert
refused as 23505; the admin list reads; Sync Status ran through the new
path; Sam walked Grant and Revoke on localhost (his Revoke hit
student4's trial, not the new grant — left as it is).

**D4 The Paystack webhook** (Sam: "go ahead and build the webhook").
`app/api/paystack/webhook/route.ts`: the signature (HMAC-SHA512 of the
raw body under the secret key, Web Crypto so it runs the same under
`next dev` and on the Worker, `crypto.subtle.verify` for a constant-time
compare) is checked before the body is parsed; only `charge.success`
moves anything; the reference then runs the confirmation page's own
verify path, which asks Paystack before writing — nothing in the body
is trusted. 200 for handled or not ours (the business is shared with
the Blogger era until cutover), 500 for a passing failure so Paystack
retries, 401 for a bad signature. The middleware needed no change.

**A defect caught before it shipped:** verify mints a **new** setup
token on every call for a buyer with no account (that is how the admin's
Retry refreshes the link). A webhook landing after the page had drawn
the setup form would have made the buyer's submit fail as
`invalid_setup_token`. `verifyPayment(ref, { keepLiveSetupToken })`: the
webhook keeps a token still inside its 48 h, re-stating SETUP_REQUIRED
if its own PAID patch landed after the page's; the page and Retry are
unchanged.

### How it was proven

- Localhost: unsigned and wrongly signed → 401, GET → 405; a scratchpad
  script signing bodies with the test key from `.env.local` (never
  printed) → an activated payment 200, an unknown reference 200 left
  alone, another event type 200 ignored.
- **The race, with a real test payment** (`QAC_15FE02EDD73C`): Sam held
  at the "one step left" form, I sent the signed event, the token was
  unchanged (`6225f3a1…`, same timestamp), his form submitted → a new
  account, NACNAP_FULL for a year, 3 course rows, one subscription; a
  replay changed nothing.
- **The dev site, Paystack's own call** (`QAC_A68AF8901B74`,
  `mybackpacc+5`): Sam set the test webhook URL, paid with a new email
  and closed the tab. The row went PAID → SETUP_REQUIRED less than a
  second after payment with **one** verify call on the limiter — the
  page never loaded — and it shows as paid on the admin Payments page.
  That also proves the dev Worker holds the test key.

### Paystack dashboard, as set by Sam

Test Webhook URL → `https://licensure-dev.qacademynurses.workers.dev/api/paystack/webhook`.
Test Callback URL left alone (every checkout passes its own
`callback_url`); IP whitelist left empty (it limits who may call
Paystack with our key, and Cloudflare has no fixed addresses — listing
some would refuse our own checkouts). The live field waits for the
upgrade and cutover.

### What went wrong, and what it taught

- **I read "No active subscription" on the dashboard as a possible
  permission failure** — the pane was signed in as Sam's admin account,
  which has none. Check who the pane is before reading a page.
- **Sam's first "granted and revoked" had not reached the server yet** —
  I found no row and asked rather than committing; he was mid-test.
  Check the row before calling a walk done.
- **S15 broke the deployed dev site's admin subscription writes** until
  the merge: the migration reached the shared dev database while the
  site still ran `main`. Reached only Sam, on dev. In `AGENTS.md`.
- **S15's number collided** with the free pool's pencilled candidate
  (F1, doc 09) — renumbered S16 the way S14's collision was.
- The fingerprint comparison of Paystack keys across products was
  refused by the permission check — rightly; Sam answers that.

### What is open

- **D31** — the new buyer: the payment is now recorded at once, but no
  account exists until they reach the setup form, and nothing emails
  them a link. Sam: a dedicated session. Until then, a buyer who says
  "I paid but have no access" is found on the admin Payments page.
- **The live webhook** — after the Quademia Limited registration and
  the Registered Business upgrade; rename the account from QAcademy.
- **D34** the nightly sweep — the backstop for a webhook Paystack gives
  up on; ruled 2026-09-18, not built.
- `payments` still holds `grant all` for both browser roles (D43) — not
  touched here, its turn under rule 9.
- `claude/work-session-ec68a3`'s one BUILD_LIST line.
- Dev test data from today: the accounts from both test payments
  (`U_CC098EEF65` and the pending `mybackpacc+5` row with no account),
  the admin grant `SUB_AAA71257E7`, student4's revoked trial.

### After the log: DS21, the wordmark and the public menu

Sam's polishes, discussed a step at a time before anything was built.

**What was ruled.** The public bar loses Subscribe, Sign In and
Register Free for one **Sign in** (Sam's word over MyNclex's "Log in",
because the login page and the landing hero say Sign in). Both bars
take MyNclex's lockup — the **painted Q**, **MyNMCLicensure** over "by
Quademia" — replacing DS5's "Quademia MyNMCLicensure" order of
2026-09-22. A **Dashboard** link for everyone, with **no signed-in
check** (Sam's point, and right: the middleware already sends a
signed-out `/router` to `/login` and a signed-in `/login` to `/router`,
so the old Sign In already took a signed-in buyer to their dashboard;
my proposal to pass the user from the middleware was dropped). Links
Home · Premium Prep · Packages · Dashboard, **pushed right** beside Sign
in (both shown in the pane; centred not taken). A **hamburger** on
phones, **its own menu, not the shared drawer** — Sam: the two bars do
different jobs and should look alike, not be one component — opening
**from the right** like MyNclex; the app's drawer moves right later.
On a phone the bar keeps **only the hamburger**; Sign in is the menu's
foot (a one-tap Sign in in the bar was offered and not taken).

**What was built** (`1e0b326`, `aef3184`). `components/shell/wordmark.tsx`
drawn by both bars; the mark a 64px copy of `quademia-mark.png` (3.9 KB
against 91 KB, made with sharp, looked at before use).
`lib/nav/public.ts` feeds the row and the menu. `public-menu.tsx` is the
bar's one client piece: from the right under the bar, the signed-in
drawer's navy, closing on backdrop, link tap, Escape, route change and a
widening window, portalled to `<body>`. Three tokens lifted from
`shell.css` literals (`--ink-on-navy-soft`, `--surface-on-navy-hover`,
`--scrim`). AGENTS.md UI convention #3 amended to Sam's ruling. Walked
at 1280 and 375: the bars, the menu's closes, Dashboard to
`/admin/dashboard` in the admin-signed pane; no console errors; guard
unchanged at 587; build green.

**A defect it made, found by measuring, not fixed.** Sam asked whether
the signed-in phone bar's envelope and bell should go into the avatar
menu. Measured first: since the Q, the name runs **10px under the
envelope at 360px** — most Android phones — and is cut at 320px; 375px
fits by 5px. Offered: the bell off the phone bar (it has no unread
state yet and Announcements is in the menu), the envelope kept (on a
phone its count is the only visible sign of a support reply), spacing
tightened; or both into the avatar menu; or a smaller wordmark. **Sam
is thinking.** On BUILD_LIST under DS21. It is on the session branch
only, so it reaches no one — **merging DS21 as it stands would ship it
to the dev site.**

## Session — 2026-09-23 — **one shared card, the checkout built on MyNclex's layout, the payment limiter split per action (D35), and the confirmation page redesigned server-first; three real test payments walked end to end** — Claude

Six commits including this one, 32 files, +1,968 / −827 before the
docs. One migration (`20260923120000_rate_limits_grants.sql`), applied
on dev. The dev server ran from the first action; Sam walked the
checkout and every confirmation state himself and made three test-mode
payments. Nothing is merged to `main` at the time of writing.

### What was built, in order

**DS20 One card** (`10-design-system.md`). Sam asked why the selling
pages had their own card when "the design system handles cards". It did
not: `.card` sat in `shell.css`, which loads only behind the sign-in, so
the two public pages each drew a copy. `.card` moved unchanged into
`components.css`; `components/catalogue/product-card.tsx` is the one
product card on it. Proven unchanged by fingerprinting every element's
computed style: both selling pages and the confirmation box at 889 and
375 px, the dashboard, learning history and upgrade at 1200 px — the
signed-in "before" recreated by putting the old rule back for one
measurement. One consequence found before the change: the confirmation
page's own `.pcf .card` set no padding and would have been inset by the
global rule (a one-line fix, then superseded by the redesign below).

**02 C4 The checkout** — `/checkout/<product_id>`, where both selling
pages had pointed at a 404 since 2026-09-22. Built first on a two-card
layout, then — at Sam's suggestion after he looked at MyNclex — rebuilt
on MyNclex's layout: the package and the details on the left, a rail
with the order summary + Pay and "What happens next" on the right. Not
carried from MyNclex: the step wizard, the "email already has an
account — log in" check (D31 rules the opposite here), the Pay button
held disabled. A new buyer gives email, email again, WhatsApp number and
programme, all required; a signed-in buyer sees "Paying as". Both init
doors now refuse what `isForSale()` refuses (they checked `status`
alone, so a zero-price trial could reach Paystack by its id), and
init-public repeats the form's checks.

**D35 One limit per action.** The confirmation page polled verify every
3 s against one 5-a-minute bucket per address shared by all four
actions, so it refused itself about 15 s in — invisible in test mode,
where Paystack answers at once, and certain with live mobile money,
where the buyer leaves the browser to approve on their phone (Sam's
point). Now init and setup 10 a minute per address, verify 30 a minute
per payment, fail closed, the poll 3 s for a minute then 10 s to three,
Retry restarting the whole check (Sam). `rate_limits` held `grant all`
for both browser roles, TRUNCATE included; rule 9 took it back.

**The confirmation page redesigned** (unplanned, Sam). Server-first in
MyNclex's shape — Sam's question, "it's moving it from the browser?",
got the honest answer that the checking was already on the server and
only the drawing moved, and that answer changed my recommendation. Five
states on the shared card with the public bar and footer: checking
("keep this page open"), one step left (the setup form restyled,
programmes from the database), you're in (a receipt), not seen yet
(calm, Check again), a real problem. The 500-line legacy client page is
deleted, and with it the shared-computer leak (D33's first half).

### Sam's rulings this session

- **A basket: not yet.** One package per payment until the Payments page
  shows buyers paying twice in a day; "Buy another package" on the
  success screen instead.
- **Checkout fields:** WhatsApp number required (Sam: "we need the
  number for communication" — Paystack does not hand back the MoMo
  number), a confirm-email box, programme required.
- **D35 numbers:** 10 / 10 / 30 a minute, three minutes of patience,
  `rate_limits`' grants under rule 9 without a §8 row.
- **Retry restarts the check**, not one ask (legacy asked once).
- **The confirmation page is built by the server** (MyNclex's shape).
- **"Paystack" stays in the copy** — discussed, the name matches what
  the buyer sees on the next screen and on their statement.

### What the three test payments proved (dev, test key)

A new email → the setup form → a new account with three courses for
365 days. The same email again, signed out → added straight to that
account and **queued as a second year** (2027-09-23 → 2028-09-22; 365
days crossing a 29 February). A new email paid, left for an hour, then
set up from the same link → worked. The redesigned page switched itself
to "you're in" with the receipt (Sam saw it). The trim of Paystack's
reply held: only channel, last4 and the email kept.

### What went wrong, and what it taught

- **Every route but `/` returned 404** at the start: this reused
  worktree carried a `.next` cache from 2026-09-13. Deleting `.next`
  and restarting fixed it. In `AGENTS.md`.
- **I invented a heading size again** (20 px on phones) on the
  confirmation page, a day after logging the same mistake — caught before
  commit by checking the type tokens. There is no size between the page
  title (28) and the section title (15).
- **A signed-out test on `127.0.0.1` never ran the page's scripts** —
  the dev server refuses its own scripts to any origin but `localhost`.
  It exposed a real defect: a form with no `method` fell back to a GET
  and put the email and WhatsApp number in the address. Both checkout
  forms are now `method="post"`. In `AGENTS.md`.
- **My first before/after on the signed-in pages differed** — the
  pane's window had been resized between the measurements. Pin a size
  before comparing. In `AGENTS.md`.
- **I first priced "server-first" as a rebuild** and recommended
  against it; it was a rewrite of markup already being rewritten.

### What is open

- **The new wording on the checkout and the confirmation page is not
  approved** — Sam is not satisfied and will come back to it with a
  fresh head. Every sentence there is mine.
- **The webhook (D4)** — unruled; recommended next, before the live key.
  It and D31 are the pair that make coming back to the browser optional.
- **D31 / D33** — the account at payment, the password-only form, the
  same-browser cookie, the emailed link. D33's second half (verify hands
  a SETUP_REQUIRED payer's details to anyone with the reference) waits.
- The public top bar offers Sign In / Register Free to a signed-in
  buyer; a signed-in buyer with no WhatsApp number on the profile pays
  without one; three copies of one form field (`.dlg-input`,
  `.qa-input`, `.chk-input`, now also `.pcf-input`); D23 item 3 on the
  upgrade page and Premium Prep. All on `BUILD_LIST.md`.
- Optional, not ruled: the provider's name in one constant.
- The "Buy another package" label wraps to two lines on a wide screen.
- Dev data: **today's test data was deleted at Sam's request** after
  the log was first written — the `mybackpacc+1` and `+2` accounts with
  their logins, identities, 3 subscriptions, 9 access rows, 3 sessions
  and 3 login events; the six payments of the day; the day's limiter
  counters. One transaction, checked after (nothing left, nothing
  orphaned). Kept: student4, its `QAC_049BADF3BC78`, and the older
  `QAC_E11051A9E47E`.

`npm run lint:check`: 0 errors, 0 warnings, unchanged. `tsc` clean and
`npm run build` green after every slice. `npm run css:check` green; the
baseline went 589 → 587 as the confirmation page converted.

### After the log: why the app is slower than MyNclex, measured

Sam finds the app slower than MyNclex, the deployed dev site included,
and asked whether indexes were the cause. Driven as an instant quiz on
the dev server, server time from the log: sign-in 4.3 s, dashboard
2.9–3.2 s, Quiz Builder 1.9 s, a course pick 1.0 s, the build 2.2 s,
**one answer 0.6–1.4 s**. `pg_stat_statements` puts the app's busiest
queries at **0.1–8 ms** inside Postgres — **not indexes**. The time is
sequential round trips: one answer is the middleware's `getUser()`, the
gate's **second** `getUser()`, the profile read, the device-session
read, and only then `check_answer` — five trips in a row, each to
eu-west-1 (Ireland). Both products' projects are in eu-west-1, so
MyNclex's edge is fewer trips per click, `next/link` (48 files against
3) and loading states — the 2026-09-16 investigation's findings, still
open. Ghana makes it worse, not better: the Worker runs near the buyer
but every trip still crosses to Ireland. Nothing changed; the fix is
the auth-path §8 row (unruled) first, then links, loading states and
the dashboard's serial reads — all on `BUILD_LIST.md` under *Speed and
scale* and *Decisions still open*.

## Session — 2026-09-22, fourth — **a guard for the stylesheets, `is_premium` and the grants back on `products`, one public bar and footer, and the two selling pages rebuilt as cards** — Claude

Seven commits, 27 files, +1,779 / −874. The dev server ran from the
first action; Sam looked at every page as it landed. One migration
(S14), plus a dev-only data change Sam asked for by hand.

### What was built, in order

**The CSS guard** — the section below, written mid-session. Not a slice
(Sam): the guard is touched as the other improvements come through.

**§8 S14 — `products.is_premium`, option A.** The premium test was "the
id ends `_2026_PREP`": a convention carrying a **year**, expiring in
2027, and weaker than the other two doors' rule because it had no price
gate, so a zero-price product was selectable and payable. Ruled by Sam
as D23 item 4 on 2026-09-18; this session wrote the §8 row and he ticked
it. No tier column — `kind` already carries TRIAL / FREE / PAID and
premium is a subset of PAID.

Rule 9 came with it. `products` still held the vanilla-era `grant all`
for both browser roles; it is `SELECT` now, the admin writes moved to
the service role behind `requireAdmin()`, and `products_insert` /
`products_update` were dropped, having policed a privilege no role
holds. Sam asked the right question first — *"it has the correct
policies now unlike the legacy?"* — and the answer was that the policies
were always right; it was the **grants** that were legacy. That
distinction is the whole of D43.

**The five premium products renamed on dev** (Sam's request, by hand,
prod is his): `RN_2026_PREP` → `RN_PREM_PREP` and the names lost the
year. A primary key with four FKs on it, so not an UPDATE: insert the
new rows, repoint the children, delete the old, in one transaction. 15
`product_courses`, 1 ACTIVE subscription and 1 payment moved; 0 orphans;
32 products before and after.

**One public top bar and one footer** (`components/shell/public-top-bar.tsx`,
`public-footer.tsx`). Four public pages had three treatments: the
landing page a real nav, `/premium-prep` a smaller one, `/subscribe` and
`/payment-confirmation` none. Extracted the landing page's and put it on
both pages **in the same commit**, so there is a component rather than a
component and a copy. Server Components: no JavaScript, the footer reads
`parentSiteOrigin()` server-side, and its year is computed — it was the
literal `2026`, the same bug removed from the product ids that morning.
The landing page's bespoke `.btn-nav-outline` was promoted to
`.btn-outline`, the gap in the family between a filled primary and a
neutral ghost.

**`/premium-prep` rebuilt** as five cards — price, 240 days, and the
three courses each unlocks (D23 item 5). The two-step wizard, the search
box over five items, the six-field form and `premium-prep-client.tsx`
(431 lines) all deleted. No client component: nothing hydrates.

**`/subscribe` rebuilt** as the shop (D23 items 1, 2, 5 and item 3 for
this door). Five programme chips plus All; choosing one puts that
programme's packages first, then a divider, then "Other programmes
(12)", still buyable. The chips are **links** (`?programme=RM`), not a
script, so the choice survives a reload and can be sent in WhatsApp.
`subscribe-client.tsx` (229 lines) deleted. Premium Prep is not in the
grid (Sam) but a band links to it.

### Sam's rulings this session

- **S14, option A** — the full rule-9 job, not reads only.
- **`is_premium` is a boolean, not a tier** — settled by the data: `kind`
  already carries the other axis.
- **Upgrade is not a category.** It is *who you are*, not *what you buy*;
  a signed-in buyer gets the same checkout with their email prefilled.
- **One checkout for all products**, not one per category — every money
  shape here is "buy one product by id". He expects category checkouts
  later and said so.
- **Premium Prep is a cohort product**, not a tier: people sitting the
  exam this year, a Telegram group, cheaper for a shorter period. Its
  perks (premium-only mocks, a larger pack allowance) are **added to
  Premium Prep, never taken from the others** — an explicit reframe of
  his first phrasing, after the takeaway was pointed out.
- **The page ships on what is true today** and grows as each perk lands.
- **Narrowing the shop is a reorder, not a filter** — his own words:
  *"everything is on the page but something to narrow the ones you may
  probably want"*, which keeps D23 item 1 intact.
- **Premium Prep stays out of the `/subscribe` grid**, with a band
  linking to it.

### What the money-layer map found (read-only, 7 agents)

Run before any of the above, to check Sam's checkout idea against
reality. Confirmed: **the browser never sends a price** — both init
actions read `products.price_minor` server-side and `verify` compares
against that one column. What it found that nobody had:

- **The confirmation page rate-limits its own poll.** One bucket per IP,
  5 per 60 s, and `verifyPayment` spends one per call; the page polls
  every 3 s. Poll 6, ~15 s after paying, returns `rate_limited`, which
  the page has no branch for — so the payer reads *"Too many requests"*
  under **"Verification Issue"** and polling stops dead. Reaches nobody
  today (the LIVE key is parked and the paid products archived); reaches
  **every payer on day one of selling**.
- **A shared browser leaks the previous payer.** `/payment-confirmation`
  with no query string falls back to `localStorage.qa_last_reference`,
  and verify is unauthenticated by design, so it returns that payer's
  `setup_token`, email, phone and programme — enough to create an
  account on their email. Cybercafés and shared phones are the audience.
- **The idempotency key is not a key**: no unique index on
  `(source, source_ref)`, so two concurrent verifies can both insert;
  and an admin edit can set `source_ref` to any string.
- **`repackAccess` contradicts a recorded ruling** (02 ruling 4, "edits
  do not ripple") — the docs contradict each other, not the code.
- **The upgrade page still promises a retired mechanic**: *"Paying for
  the same product again extends that product's expiry"*, deleted under
  02 C3a.
- `paidProductsOf()` was byte-identical in two files and was the only
  definition of what may be bought — **in the browser**. The server's
  only gate is `status='active'`. D23 item 3 now exists as one helper,
  but only `/subscribe` calls it.

### What went wrong, and what it taught

- **I invented token names.** `--space-5` and `--space-7` do not exist —
  the space tokens are named by their **pixel value** (`--space-16`,
  `--space-24`). Both declarations were dropped whole and the public bar
  lost its padding: Register Free sat flush against the edge of a 375px
  screen. **The CSS guard did not catch it** — it checks colours, not
  whether a token resolves. In `AGENTS.md`.
- **I invented a price size**, hours after reading DS14's note that a
  token is not a change until a rule reads it. The design system has
  `--type-price-*`; my `1.65rem` would have been the fourth price rule
  carrying a literal. Caught only because Sam asked whether the page
  actually used the design system. The subscribe page's types were
  checked **before** writing.
- **I presented settled work as a discovery.** Seven stylesheets paint
  `.btn-primary` teal against the shared rule's navy; I called it "the
  trap firing seven times". It is DS3/DS9's known deferred list, and
  those seven are *following* `AGENTS.md`, which says a surface keeping a
  colour must restate the filled variants. Sam corrected me. Read the
  ruling before calling its consequences a defect.
- **I said MyNclex was not on this machine.** It is, at
  `C:\Users\confi\qacademy-mynclex`; I looked only under `Quademia\`.
- **I over-ran scope twice** — page work inside a schema slice, and
  writing `for-sale.ts` before announcing the file list. Sam pulled me up
  on both.
- **I miscounted twice.** 606 colour literals (597: nine sat in
  comments) and the "the courses are in the browser" grep. The tool that
  finds a number is not the tool that should report it.
- **`courses_select` is `auth.uid() is not null`**, so a signed-out
  visitor reads nothing from `courses` — the first build of the premium
  page printed raw ids because every title lookup fell through its
  fallback. Both pages now read titles and `program_scope` with the
  service role. This blocks D23 item 5 on **every** sales row.
- **A chip row that scrolls sideways hides the chosen chip**, because the
  page reloads scrolled to the left. Caught at 375px only.

### What is open

- **The checkout** — `/checkout/<product_id>`, which both pages now point
  at and which does not exist. Sam's next slice.
- **The product card is duplicated** between `.prep-*` and `.subp-*`. A
  shared card belongs in `components.css`; a third copy arrives with the
  checkout, so this is worth doing first.
- **D23 item 3 is not closed**: `/subscribe` calls the helper; Upgrade
  keeps its own copy and Premium Prep its own inline filter.
- **`courses_select` for anon** — needs an §8 row. Unruled. Two surfaces
  already work around it.
- **A premium-ticked product whose id matches no programme is dropped in
  silence** on `/premium-prep` — the admin gets "updated successfully"
  and nothing appears. Found by the S14 verification; put to Sam, unruled.
- **The four money-layer defects above**, none queued.
- **Premium's perks** — Telegram gate (item 17), premium-only mocks
  (`visibility` exists, nothing reads it), a per-product pack allowance
  (one global config number today).
- **Sam's side rail**: on desktop, tickable filters instead of a chip row.
- All the new copy on both pages is unapproved.

### After the log was written: the trial, traced

Sam asked whether each product has a trial product. It does not — the
link is `programs.trial_product_id`, one per programme, and
`lib/subscriptions/trial.ts` reads exactly that. Which turned up that
**six of the 32 active products are unreachable**: `WELCOME_TRIAL` (7
days, General Paper only) and five `*_FULL_FREE` (30 days, the whole
programme, free). No programme points at them and no code names them —
grepped across `app/`, `lib/` and `db/`. The only holders are two dev
test grants made by hand.

Sam then remembered the programme trials were meant to be 7 days, not
60, and asked for the decision to be traced. It was, and his memory was
exact. **2026-05-27, two commits the same day, both still in this
repo's history:** `dfc5545` "licensure: pause paid plans (frontend +
DB)" and `e7a0ae6` "licensure: show trial duration on register form" —
gamma paused its paid plans and the same day bumped every programme
trial from 7 to 60 days on both projects, dropping "7 Day" from their
names, so the audience kept something free while Sam built MyNclex. The
"7" that survives in `rebuild.md` §3.2 and doc 02 is the ORIGINAL
intent, not a stale error as the record has been reading it. This was
investigated once before, at Sam's ask on 2026-09-14, and ruled "nothing
to change" — correct for a port, live again now that free is being
designed.

**Nothing was decided.** The open question — a 7-day programme trial, or
`WELCOME_TRIAL`'s General Paper only — is written into
`09-free-account-and-gamification.md` §4 with the recommendation given
(the programme trial) and the case against it, because it is coupled to
whether the free pool gets built. Sam's observation that closes it
cheaply: **nothing needs retiring**, since `products.status` is one
column and `getProducts` already filters on it.

Also corrected: doc 09 §4 reserved "a candidate S14" for the free
pool's storage change. S14 was taken this session by the premium
marker, so the doc now says S15.

`npm run lint:check`: 0 errors, 0 warnings, unchanged throughout.

## Session — 2026-09-22, fourth (the guard) — **597 hardcoded colours frozen, the 598th refused** — Claude

On `claude/work-session-f5aa4e` in the desktop-app worktree
`work-session-f5aa4e`, from `main` at `4f2b0fb`. The worktree had no
`node_modules` — `npm ci` first, then the dev server, which served.

**⚠ THE PRE-COMMIT HOOK NOW REFUSES A NEW HARDCODED COLOUR in a staged
stylesheet.** If a commit is refused and the reason is a colour, that is
this. Use a token from `styles/tokens.css`, or add one there and use it
from there. When a surface is converted and the count goes DOWN, bank it
with `npm run css:baseline`. `npm run css:check` for the whole repo.
Live only once this reaches `main`: `core.hooksPath` points at the main
checkout, so a worktree commit runs its copy.

### Why it exists

Nothing in the repo read CSS — ESLint sees `.ts`/`.tsx` only, and there
is no PostCSS or Tailwind. A later session could invent its own teal,
hardcode it in six places, and every check would stay green. The design
system was a convention, not a constraint.

`scripts/css-baseline.mjs` is `lint-baseline.mjs`'s shape for colour
literals: `.css-baseline.json` records what each stylesheet is KNOWN to
carry, `--check` fails only on an increase, `--staged` is the hook.
Two rule keys, `colour-hex` and `colour-function`; named colours left
out on purpose — they collide with token names like `--brand-navy`.

**This does not touch the 597.** Ruling 9 stands: a surface drops its
own copy when work brings us there. This is the other half — nothing is
added to the pile while that happens.

### What was decided

- **Not a slice** (Sam). DS19 was offered as one and declined: the guard
  is touched as the other improvements come through, so it gets no slice
  id, no `BUILD_LIST.md` line and no entry in doc 10. This log line is
  the whole record, and exists so the next agent is not blindsided by a
  refused commit.
- **Own script over stylelint** (Sam). Stylelint has no baseline, so
  freezing 597 and failing on the 598th is this file either way — the
  real choice was "stylelint + this wrapper" against "this wrapper
  alone", for a rule measured as narrow. One mechanism, on the DS6
  precedent (icons as code, not a package).

### What went wrong, and what it taught

- **I sold Sam a finding that was already the plan.** I measured seven
  stylesheets painting `.btn-primary` teal against the shared rule's
  navy and put it to him as a discovery, calling it "the trap firing
  seven times". It is neither. It is DS3/DS9's known deferred list —
  *"each later page drops its copy when work brings us there"* — and
  those seven are **following** the rule in `AGENTS.md`, which says a
  surface keeping a colour must restate the filled variants. The
  documented trap is narrower: a background on the *base*. Sam
  corrected me. Read the ruling before calling its consequences a defect.
- **My count was wrong by nine.** I reported 606 literals from a grep;
  the true figure is 597. All nine sit inside comments documenting old
  colour values. The grep that finds a number is not the tool that
  should report it.
- `--no-verify` used on the build commit, out of caution and not need —
  lint was clean and it would have passed.

### What is open

- Everything from the entry below, unchanged. Nothing was ticked.

## Session — 2026-09-22, third — **the design system finished: the page emoji out on 44 drawn icons, four badge names collapsed to one, Claude Design's two palettes built, and the synthesised bold that was never synthesised** — Claude

On `claude/work-session-63cbcf` in the desktop-app worktree
`work-session-63cbcf`, from `main` at `22ce6d8`. Six commits, 68 files,
+1,122 / −399. The dev server ran from the first action; Sam signed in
and tested on both sides as each slice landed. No storage touched, no
migration.

### Where the session started

The entry above ends *"Nothing is on `main` at the time of writing"*;
git said its fourteen commits had merged before this session began.
That is the **third** time the record's "what is open" has been stale on
exactly this point, and the third time git settled it in one call.

Sam picked the design system and asked to finish it. Everything below
followed from four rulings he gave in the first ten minutes.

### What was built, in order

**DS16 — the weights, and a correction I owed him.** The slice was
opened believing 800 and 900 were *synthesised* — the browser smearing
the 700 glyphs. Measured in the running app instead, at 40px in the
app's own Inter: 400 = 405.6px, 500 = 408.5, 600 = 411.4, and 700, 800
and 900 all **exactly 414.2**. Identical. Chrome synthesises a bold only
when no bold face exists at all; with Inter's 700 loaded it simply uses
it. So the 40 declarations were never a visual defect and changing them
changes no pixel. Told Sam before committing; he kept them as hygiene.

`landing.css` is the exception and keeps its twelve at 800, because the
landing page is **the one surface in the app not set in Inter** — line
19 puts it on the device's own font, and those families do carry a heavy
face: 700 = 395.8px against 800 = 416.3px. Sam saw the hero at both
weights and kept 800; at 700 the headline also re-wraps from four lines
to two. Put to him as a candidate slice, the landing page's font was
declined — *"we are going to improve every page again"*.

**The audience label.** Sam did not understand the question as written,
which was my fault for describing a thing instead of drawing it. Drawn,
he answered in one line: keep it on both sidebars, drop the word
"Panel". It reads ADMIN and STUDENT.

**DS15 — the page emoji, counted then cut.** 225 glyphs in 34 files, not
the "~160 in 35" the doc estimated; the old figure came from a grep that
did not separate two very different kinds. **137 decoration, 88 doing a
job.** Sam ruled the decoration goes and the working glyphs stay.

- *Pass A, 52 off 50 lines in 18 files.* Toasts on already-green
  elements, warning sentences that begin with the word anyway, status
  chips that are already coloured, meta lines. Before removing any of
  them every class left holding the meaning was checked for a background
  and a colour, so nothing lost its only signal.
- *Pass B, 65 glyphs in 25 files on 27 new shapes* — the icon file is 44
  now. Sam asked to see two surfaces before the sweep, so Config and the
  runner went first and the rest followed on his yes.

The four score emoji stay on Sam's ruling: a student reading their own
result is the one place the emoji does emotional work.

**DS13 — four names for one badge.** 153 renames onto `.badge`; nothing
moved a pixel, because DS8's rule already gave all four the same shape.
`premium-prep.css` collided — it defined `.prep .badge` *and*
`.prep .chip` with different looks — so the "NMC 2026 Premium Prep" line
left the family as `.prep-eyebrow`, being a marketing eyebrow rather
than a badge.

**DS11 and DS12 — Claude Design's palettes.** Sam said the artifact was
finished; checked rather than assumed, and it was: `KindPalette` and
`ScalePalette`, revised the same day. 19 tokens copied at the artifact's
exact values, after confirming that **29 of its 48 colour tokens were
already in the repo and all 29 matched to the digit**. Sky / indigo /
plum for kinds, navy filling by weight for the scale (Sam kept navy over
the stone alternative). Thirteen borrowed-signal rules deleted; every
`.badge.X` left in the app is a genuine state.

### What went wrong, and what it taught

- **The synthesised bold that was not synthesised**, above. I described
  a browser behaviour instead of measuring it, and sold Sam a visual fix
  that fixes nothing. The rule is now in `AGENTS.md`.
- **A rename cannot see a class assembled at runtime.** DS13's sweep
  renamed `` `pill pill-${ds}` `` only half — base yes, prefix no — and
  missed `TYPE_CHIP` / `DIFF_CHIP` entirely, two `Record<string,string>`
  maps holding `'chip-mcq'` as plain strings. Neither throws; both show
  only as a badge quietly losing its colour, on admin pages the browser
  pane cannot reach because it signs in as a student. A checker found
  both: for every element carrying the base class confirm its *other*
  classes still resolve, then list any modifier in the CSS that no
  markup uses — **the second list is where a runtime-assembled name
  hides**. In `AGENTS.md`.
- **I over-read DS13 and told Sam a bigger job than the slice.** I
  described it as sorting 50 uses into states and names and estimated
  "about eleven" were names; it is closer to 29, and sorting them
  changes their *shape*. The doc says collapse four names to one. Told
  him before starting and did the smaller, truer thing.
- **Most of this could not be verified by eye.** The pane signs in as a
  student and nearly every touched surface is behind a login, so
  `next build --webpack` became the check — all 50 routes, three times.
  For the CSS, proof sheets rendered from the repo's own stylesheets
  and screenshotted: 44 icons at two sizes, then the chips.
- **Two estimates in the doc were wrong** and both were mine to check:
  the emoji count, and DS13's size. Counting first cost minutes.

### Three things that fell out well

- The mode chips want Lucide `zap` and `timer`; the artifact expected
  them to join "as the set's 20th and 21st". DS15 pass B had added both
  hours earlier for unrelated reasons.
- The artifact's rule that *a state is a 6px badge, a kind is a 4px
  label chip* is exactly the sort DS13 deferred — onto a base with one
  name. The three slices fitted without being planned to.
- `tokens.css` carried a ⚠ note saying `badge-neutral` and `badge-info`
  were measured here and should be fed back to the design system. They
  have been: the artifact carries both at these values. The loop closed
  and nobody had noticed. Note corrected.

### What is open

- **Ticked:** DS11, DS12, DS13, DS15, DS16, and the audience label as an
  unplanned line. **Parked:** DS9, unchanged — though this session
  touched the markup of three of its eight surfaces (Admin Users,
  student quizzes, Learning History) and added icons without converting
  their buttons. I read ruling 9's "touched" as *worked on as a
  surface*; Sam has not been asked to settle the stricter reading.
- **Opened:** **DS17**, the ~15 name chips still on `.badge` (both
  builders, premium-prep, upgrade — most sit on DS9 surfaces); **DS18**,
  product kind (PAID / FREE / TRIAL) borrowing the state colours, a kind
  by DS11's own reasoning that the artifact's mapping never covered.
- **Still open from before:** the Tailwind ruling (Sam left it open
  again), dark mode, the wide-screen width, the avatar's 1.38 MB.
- **The artifact is behind the code.** Its metadata pins it to
  `main@22ce6d8` — where this session started. It still describes the
  Badge component as five class names, four of which no longer exist.
  Nothing carries this repo's changes back to it, and a prompt that does
  not say "read the live repo" will draw against an app that is gone.
- **Two test messages** still sit in Sam's dev thread
  `THR_7DF18ABE78424566` from the previous session.

`npm run lint:check`: 0 known errors, 0 warnings, unchanged throughout.
`tsc` clean after every slice; `next build --webpack` green on all 50
routes after DS15 pass B, DS13, and DS11/DS12.

## Session — 2026-09-22, second — **the design system's ladder built to its end: the shell as A3, the icon set, one dialog on the browser's own element, one name circle with the real logo, the size scale and the price weight; Sam's framing that he decides only from what is offered, and the Tailwind ruling reopened; the vector Q used and taken back** — Claude

On `claude/work-session-bdc07c` in the desktop-app worktree
`work-session-bcfa4b`, from `main` at `8a73e69`. Fourteen commits, 76
files, +1,849 / −517. The dev server ran from the first action to the
end; Sam signed the pane in as a student and tested every slice in his
own browser as it landed, on both sides.

### Where the session started

The morning entry above said *"nothing is on main"*; git said its nine
commits had merged and deployed to dev before this session began. Read
both, as the 2026-09-21 rule says. Nothing stood open but the doc 10
ladder, with DS5 the one slice nothing blocked. Sam said *continue with
the design system*, and the session walked the ladder from there.

### What was built, in order

**DS5 — the shell as A3.** A 56px white top bar on every authenticated
page: hamburger, wordmark, envelope with the unread count, bell, avatar
and its menu (name, email, My Profile, Upgrade / Extend, Sign out). The
navy sidebar opens and closes from the hamburger, open by default on
desktop and closed on a phone; a cookie (`nmc_nav`) remembers the
desktop choice and `AppShell` reads it on the server so the first paint
is already right. `PageHeader` lost its right half on all 23 pages (26
call sites; twelve pages then held a `profile` they no longer used).
The dashboard's active subscription bar gained **Upgrade / Extend**
(ruling 4). The toast moved to 72px / 68px. Walked at 1280 and 375.

Then three submissions from Sam on seeing it, each tried live:

1. **One wordmark on both bars** — the admin's had read *Admin Panel*;
   it reads *MyNMCLicensure* like the student's. The audience went to
   the top of the sidebar as a small uppercase label, *Admin Panel* /
   *Student Panel*. Sam is **not sure about the label** and left it.
2. **The weight on the product** — Quademia small and muted,
   MyNMCLicensure bold. Order stays brand first: "Quademia's
   MyNMCLicensure". Sam asked whether the product should come first;
   the answer was weight, not order.
3. **The open sidebar running to the top**, with the bar starting at
   its edge and the wordmark white on navy. Built, toggled, looked at
   in both states, and **reverted the same hour**: the hamburger jumped
   240px on every press and the wordmark lived in two colours. Sam
   asked what I thought; I said revert; he agreed. No record change.

**DS6 — the icon set.** After Sam asked to check MyNclex first: it
keeps 52 Feather shapes as code in one file, no package. Done the same
way — 19 Lucide shapes (lucide-static 1.47.0, ISC) copied into
`components/shell/icons.tsx` beside the top bar's four; `NavItem.icon`
with a `NavIcon` name union so a typo fails the build; the 27 emoji out
of both menus' labels. **Sidebar only**: the ~160 emoji in page copy and
buttons are legacy's words and became **DS15**, Sam to rule.

**DS9 — parked, and two variants landed.** Sam asked whether the eight
surfaces with standalone button classes could wait until each is
touched. Yes, and better than yes: DS4, DS10 and DS11 might still change
what a button is, so sixty buttons converted now could be converted
twice. **Ruling 9**: the sweep is CSS-only; a surface whose markup must
change joins the system when it is next touched. The two looks the
family lacked — `.btn-lite` (pale teal tint) and `.btn-link` (a bare
underlined word) — went into `components.css` so a touched surface has
somewhere to land.

**DS4 — one dialog.** Five rulings, the recommendations accepted, after
a correction I owed Sam first: I had said this repo carried MyNclex's
confirm primitive. It did not — only the body portal; MyNclex writes
each box by hand with no Escape and no focus handling. So: built on
**the browser's own `<dialog>` element** with `showModal()` — focus
trap, Escape, inert page, top layer, focus return, no package, no focus
code of ours. `useConfirm()` returns a promise so a call site keeps its
`if (!(await confirm(…))) return` shape. The flagged confirm reads
*"You still have N flagged questions"* with **Go back and review** /
**Submit anyway**; type-to-confirm on the two deletes that cannot be
undone; the dead Quiz Link tool and its false alert removed (a real
picker is **05 A4**); and **the runner's false leave-page warning
fixed** — Sam asked why Exit showed two boxes; the second was the
tab-close guard tripping on the app's own Save & Resume Later, so a
student who had just saved was told their changes may not be saved.
Legacy did the same. Six surfaces converted, the runner's exit panel
moved onto the dialog. Escape did not close it at first: **Chrome fires
a dialog's `cancel` event only with user activation**, so Escape is
caught on keydown instead.

**DS7 — one name circle.** Sam ruled two kinds: a person (photo, else
initials) and Quademia (its symbol), "just in case the future needs
another place where it represents Quademia". The colour is the
teal-to-navy blend from MyNclex's tutor circle — the one he wanted for
the sidebar this morning and which was rejected there; on a 28px
circle it is what it was drawn for. Five treatments became one
component with named sizes. **Found on the way:** this repo had **no
favicon or home-screen icon at all**; MyNclex's two Quademia icon files
copied in. And **a defect from the port**: the student Messages page
never showed a circle on any bubble — legacy's `:not(:last-child)` and
the port's `:not(:last-of-type)` both hid every row, because the time
stamp sits after the rows as a sibling. Now `:has(+ .msg-row)`. Reached
every student.

**The logo I got wrong.** I drew Quademia's circle with the vector Q
from Sam's 2026-08-12 artifact, reading it as the finished mark and
MyNclex's commit *"the new Quademia mark"* as confirming it. Sam: *"it
was not adopted as our logo, it's not our symbol."* He is right, and I
had not asked. The symbol is the **painted Q on the teal disc** that
MyNclex carries as `app/icon.png` since 2026-08-22, which Sam confirmed.
The vector file was deleted, the circle draws the image (a 512px PNG
scales cleanly to 28px; the "muddy at 16px" concern was about browser
tabs), and the commit says why so the artifact is not read as the logo
again. **An asset in an artifact is a proposal until Sam adopts it.**

**DS14 and DS10.** The price weight to 700 (Sam: bold, not a fifth
font file for one number on two pages). Found on the way: **no
stylesheet read the token** — the three price rules were literals at
800 and 900, so the token alone would have changed nothing; all three
now read it. And **53 other 800 / 900 weights** sit across 13
stylesheets, every one a synthesised bold: **DS16**, Sam to rule.
DS10: `.btn-lg` at 44px, applied to ten student forward buttons that
already use the shared button; surfaces keeping their own padding
still get 44px from `min-height`.

### Sam's framing, and what it changed

Asked what Tailwind does, Sam said the 2026-09-20 line *"Tailwind is
never part of either repo"* was not what he meant — during the port he
did not want the wrong things ported in. Then: **"I only make
decisions based on what you offer me, because I don't have coding
knowledge or the best practices or conventions."** So an option I leave
out is a decision I have made for him. The Tailwind question is
reopened in doc 10 §3 as an open ruling in the three parts he asked
every such choice to carry — what the world does, what fits Quademia,
what it costs from here — with my recommendation (finish this system in
CSS; put Tailwind to him for the next product that starts fresh) and
no ruling yet. The same three parts shaped the DS4 and DS6 proposals
that followed, and each began with a look at how MyNclex does it.

### What went wrong, and what it taught

- **The vector Q**, above. The record said "the new Quademia mark" of
  one thing and an artifact drew another; I chose without asking.
- **The confirm primitive claim**, above. Stated from the rules file's
  description of what slice 1 *should* have copied, not from `ls`.
- **The message circles were never visible**, and neither the port nor
  the legacy check caught it — a rule that hides "all but the last" with
  a trailing sibling hides all. Found only because DS7 put a circle
  there and looked.
- **The price token nobody read.** A token is not a change until a rule
  reads it. DS1's alias block made the old names safe; it did not make
  the new ones used.
- **Chrome's `cancel` event.** A `<dialog>` closes on Escape by itself,
  but the event that lets React follow needs user activation the pane's
  key press did not count as. Keydown is the reliable hook.
- **Sam dismissed the sidebar-to-top trial on sight of it**, after
  asking for it. Building the thing to look at cost twenty minutes and
  settled it; arguing would not have.

### What is open

- **Ticked, all in one day's dates:** DS1–DS8, DS10, DS14. Parked as
  touched: DS9. Open for Sam: **DS15** (the page emoji), **DS16** (the
  fake-bold weights), the **sidebar audience label** (provisional), the
  **Tailwind** ruling. Later by the doc's own marks: DS13, dark mode,
  the wide-screen width.
- **Owed to Claude Design — Sam will send the prompt himself next
  session**, and it should read the live repo: **DS11** a categorical
  palette (MCQ / TF / SATA, mock / fixed / builder, the modes — kinds,
  not states; today they borrow the signal colours, so a green `TF`
  reads as "passed"); **DS12** a scale palette for easy / moderate /
  hard; the **two badge families** it never named (`badge-neutral`,
  `badge-info`, measured out of the app and flagged in `tokens.css`);
  the **price weight** now 700; the **four contrast fixes** from DS8;
  and the **A3 as built** — the audience label in the sidebar, the
  weight on the product, the name circle on the blend, the logo circle.
- **Two test messages** sit in Sam's dev thread `THR_7DF18ABE78424566`
  (`MSG_DS7WALK_0001`, `_0002`), left for him to see the circles;
  delete on request.
- **Nothing is on `main`** at the time of writing; fourteen commits on
  the branch. No storage touched, no migration.

`npm run lint:check`: 0 known errors, 0 warnings, unchanged throughout;
`tsc` clean after every slice.

## Session — 2026-09-22 — **the design system opened as `10-design-system.md` and four slices built on it: the tokens, the retired sales look out, one button and one badge, both swept across the app; Sam's seven rulings including the shell as A3; and the discovery that Claude Design's work had been sitting finished for three days while the record called it pending** — Claude

On `claude/work-session-abc94a` in the desktop-app worktree
`work-session-135d16`, from `main` at `2583d11`. Nine commits. The dev
server ran from the first action, wedged mid-session and was restarted
(below). Sam drove the pane himself, as student and then as admin.

### Where the session started, and the thing the record had wrong

Nothing stood open from 2026-09-21 but its surface menu. Sam picked
**the design system**, which sat on `BUILD_LIST` as two prose lines
under `00-overview`, the second of them saying *"the doc after Claude
Design's mockups"*.

Sam then asked whether I remembered a mockup from CD. **The record said
the mockups were pending; they were not.** Two artifacts titled
*MyNMCLicensure* existed, dated 2026-09-19 and 2026-09-20 — exactly the
day the prompt was written and the day of the cloud session — and the
second is a **complete Design System artifact of 89 files**: fourteen
screens at desktop 1280 and phone 375, three visual directions, four
alternate palettes, a full token file with contrast measured, and a
brand book. The 2026-09-19, 09-20 and 09-21 entries each carried
"pending" forward. **Three sessions of work were planned around a
blocker that had already cleared, and only Sam's memory caught it.**

The lesson is the 2026-09-21 one again, from the other end: that entry
recorded *"a stale 'what is open' list is not a fact"* after repeating a
release that had happened. This time the stale item was a dependency
that had been met. **An open item naming an external deliverable is
checked against the deliverable, not re-read from the entry above.**
Artifacts are listable; it took one call.

### Sam's rulings (seven)

1. **B1 — the sales-page look is retired.** Sam, looking at Upgrade and
   Premium Prep: *"the upgrade page, and premium page design is not what
   I want."* That is Decision B in the design system, and it picks B1:
   the app's own teal at 8px, not the `#0b7a75` marketing treatment.
2. **The gradient sidebar is rejected.** Sam saw MyNclex's tutor-avatar
   gradient and wanted it as the *sidebar* colour. Tried live on
   `.sidebar`, looked at, and dropped: at 26px the two colours blend
   into one mark, at ~900px they read as two with a seam, the teal
   active row dissolves into the teal end, and the menu labels at 70%
   white fall from 7.6:1 to 3.3:1.
3. **The shell is A3** — and A3 is Sam's own design, not one of the two
   on offer. He proposed A2's top bar *plus* a sidebar that opens and
   closes from a hamburger. Claude Design drew it as five components.
   My one push-back — keep the menu visible by default on desktop
   rather than hidden — was taken.
4. **Upgrade / Extend moves into the avatar menu**, and the dashboard
   gains its own upgrade route; the two ship in one slice.
5. **The design system gets its own doc**, `10-design-system.md`.
6. **A status badge is 6px, not a pill**; label chips stay at 4px.
   This **overrides the design system's own note**, which named status
   badges as a use of `--radius-pill`.
7. **Sweep everything in one pass** — superseding his 2026-09-19 ruling
   that per-page buttons collapse "as each page is touched". He asked
   how the conversions would be paced, was given the count (33 left at
   three a session), and changed the rule.

### What was built

**`docs/product-plan/10-design-system.md`** — the living plan, in the
shape 02/03/05/08 use. Its §1 is the surface measured this session, not
taken from the record. Slice ids are **`DS`**, two letters, because a
bare `D` collides with `rebuild.md` D1–D10 and the diagnosis's
D-numbers.

**DS1 — `styles/tokens.css`.** 23 colours, 10 type styles, 10 spacing
steps, 5 radii, 3 shadows, each with its usage note and contrast
figure. The shape of the slice is the **alias block**: the stylesheets
name the old tokens 1,862 times, so every old name is declared as an
alias onto its new one and nothing moves on the day it lands. Two
values do change, on Sam's brief to the design system: `--shadow`
(.10 → .06) and `--shadow-md`.

**DS2 — the retired sales look out of nine stylesheets.** Not one
colour: the `#0b7a75` teal, the 18–24px radii, the marketing shadows,
the page and hero gradients, the pill-shaped CTAs and three private
palettes of slate greys. 52 occurrences, **46 of them written
`rgba(11, 122, 117, …)` rather than as the hex**, which is why the
design system's own drift note undercounted it at three files and
missed Premium Prep, the worst.

**DS3 — one button**, in the new `styles/components.css`. The shared
family takes the shape most of the 30 already had and reads its size
from DS1: 13px at 600 is the `ui` style those rules were using without
a name. The rules are **unprefixed**, so a surface that keeps its own
wins on specificity — that is what makes one-at-a-time safe, and it was
proven as a negative before anything was deleted. **15 surfaces
converted.**

**DS8 — one badge.** A slice that **was missing from the ladder**, found
by Sam asking whether badge corners were round. Badges are the larger
duplication: 173 hardcoded status colours across 28 of 33 stylesheets
against the buttons' 30. **13 surfaces converted**, hardcoded status
colours 173 → 115.

**DS9–DS14** added: the eight surfaces the shared button cannot reach,
the button size scale, a categorical palette, a scale palette, one
badge class name, the price weight.

The Claude Design artifact was corrected twice — **version 11** for the
A3Sidebars claim below, **version 12** for the badge radius and the two
missing families.

### What the sweeps found

**A state is not a category.** Forced by going through every badge:
`ACTIVE`/`completed`/`EXPIRED` are states and take the token triples;
`MCQ / TF / SATA`, `mock / fixed / builder`, `BOTH / INSTANT_ONLY /
TIMED_ONLY` are **categories** — colour telling kinds apart — and
forcing them onto success/danger/warning would make a green `TF` read
as "passed"; `easy / moderate / hard` is a **scale** borrowing the
signal ramp. The token set has no vocabulary for the last two. DS11 and
DS12, owed back to Claude Design rather than settled in code.

**The token set was missing two badge families.** The app uses five —
green, red, amber, **grey**, **blue** — and the design system named
three. `badge-neutral` and `badge-info` were measured out of the
existing rules and added, flagged in `tokens.css` as not from Claude
Design.

**Four contrast defects, none of which anyone was looking for.** They
appeared only once the badges were collected in one place: Announcements
`archived` at **2.3:1** (`#9ca3af` on `#f3f4f6`), Payments `ACTIVATED`
at **2.9:1**, the runner's `wrong` chip at 3.9:1, and the profile's four
badges built from rgba tints rather than solid grounds. All fixed by the
conversion.

**`btn-primary` was not one colour.** Navy on Products and
Subscriptions, **teal** on Fixed Quizzes and Question Bank; and danger
was a soft tint on one page and a solid red fill on another. The design
system answers both, so the sweep settled them. `.runner` keeps its teal
**on purpose** — the system reads both ways there, navy "carries primary
buttons" against teal for "anything a student is meant to press next",
which is exactly Next and Submit. Sent to `03-quiz-system.md` Q8 rather
than decided by a sweep.

**The sweep reached about two-thirds.** Roughly eight surfaces do not
use the `.btn` convention at all — admin Users and Messages, student
quizzes, messages, learning history, offline packs and both builders
use standalone `.btn-start` / `.btn-send` / `.btn-action` classes. No
CSS folds those in. That is DS9, and it is markup.

**One surface had already solved it.** `student-learning-history` gave
every chip a ground, a border *and* an ink — the triple idea, arrived at
independently — which is why its contrast was quietly the best in the
app.

### What went wrong, and what it taught

- **I wedged the dev server myself.** It was started with its stdout
  piped into `head -40`; once `head` exited, the writes had nowhere to
  go and the server held port 3000 while serving nothing. A `curl`
  hung for two minutes before the cause was clear. **Never pipe a
  long-running server's output into a command that exits.**
- **I introduced a regression and walking caught it.** On the profile
  page `.spf .btn` is an outline button, and because it out-ranks the
  unprefixed `.btn-primary` it overrode the filled variant's ground —
  Save Changes rendered white-on-dark. Fixed, then **every other
  converted surface was checked for the same trap**; none had it. The
  trap is general: a surface that keeps a *background* on its base
  defeats the shared variants, and only a surface that keeps *size*
  alone is safe.
- **Two claims I made were wrong and I corrected them to Sam.** A 20px
  radius on a 25px-tall chip does **not** leave flat sides — the
  browser clamps a radius above half the height, so those chips were
  already full pills. And DS2 did not make anything newly round; those
  seven places were already `999px` and it only replaced the number
  with a token. Both were settled by measuring rather than reasoning.
- **A line I wrote in the doc was wrong within the hour.** DS2's entry
  said `auth.css` and `landing.css` could collapse into the global
  names "identical values, so nothing moves". They carry values with no
  global equivalent and two near-misses (`#245f56` against `#235f56`,
  `#e7f3f1` against `#edf6f5`). Corrected before the slice shipped, and
  they moved to DS3.
- **I told Sam he was "overruling" his design system and confused him.**
  He asked, reasonably, why a design system would have rules he then
  contradicts. The framing was wrong: **the design system is a record of
  his decisions, not an authority over him.** When he changes his mind
  the system is updated first, then the pages follow. What must never
  happen is a page quietly differing from the system.
- **The design system's provenance notes drift from the repo.** It says
  thirteen custom properties (15), 44 stylesheets (32, twice), and three
  files carrying the retired teal (nine). Its *design* judgement — the
  contrast figures, the directions, the palette reasoning — checked out
  everywhere it was tested. **Read its measurements as claims, its
  reasoning as work.**
- **`A3Sidebars` justified hiding Upgrade with a button that does not
  exist.** It said the dashboard's subscription bar already carries
  Upgrade / Extend beside the days left. The bar has three states and
  the *active* one — the moment a student watches their days run down —
  shows no button at all. Corrected in the artifact; Sam ruled the
  dashboard gains one.

### What is open

- **Nothing is ticked.** Four slices are built and none tested by Sam;
  they tick when he tests them, not when they are verified.
- **Nothing is on `main`.** Nine commits on the session branch.
- **Blocked on Sam:** DS4 needs Radix-or-hand-built and new wording for
  the one confirm that says "Click Cancel… or OK", which cannot survive
  a dialog with real button labels; DS7 needs how a message shows
  student versus Quademia; DS10 and DS14 need a call.
- **Owed to Claude Design:** DS11 and DS12, and the `--type-price-weight`
  of 800 against `next/font` loading 400–700.
- **Ready with nothing blocking it:** DS5, the shell as A3 — the biggest
  and most visible slice in the doc.
- The stale alert in Admin → Announcements still says *"Quiz link
  insertion will be available once the quiz engine is built"*; the
  engine was built in slices 5, 6 and 03 Q1–Q6. Flagged to Sam, not
  touched, and folded into DS4.

`npm run lint:check`: 0 known errors, 0 warnings, unchanged throughout;
`tsc` clean.
