## Session — 2026-09-21, second — **the record made navigable: every queued `BUILD_LIST` line moved under the surface that will build it, the three flat lists gone; the diagnosis-to-doc link verified whole by script; the prod release of the evening confirmed from the run log** — Claude

On `claude/load-yourself-lets-world-1a77b8` in the desktop-app worktree
`work-session-90f627`, from `main` at `80b1c7d`. Two commits, docs only;
no code, no migration, no walk. The dev server ran from the first action
to the end. Sam never signed the pane in — nothing needed it.

### Where the session started

Nothing stood open from the session above but its own list — and one
item on that list was already closed without either of us saying so.
**The three migrations it names as pending went to prod the same
evening**: PR #3 merged `main` → `production` at 22:12 UTC, both prod
jobs green, the runner reporting *21 applied, 3 pending (24 on disk)*
and then `attempt_items`, `attempt_answers` and
`question_bank_secret_half` each "done". Prod is level with dev at 24
migrations and `git log origin/production..origin/main` is empty. The
entry above is not wrong — it records what was true when it was written,
which is the rule — but it is not current, and I read it as if it were.
Sam corrected me. **The record says what was true then; git says what is
true now, and the two are checked separately.** Nothing was written down
about the release, by design: `SESSIONS.md` and `BUILD_LIST.md` both
forbid release status because git and the run log are the truth.

### Sam's two questions, and what they found

**"Are the plan docs not containing the planned areas?"** Checked, not
answered from memory. **Five docs are living plans** with a §4 and a
ladder — 02, 03, 05, 08, 09. **Five are not** — 00, 01, 04, 06, 07 still
describe the legacy product, and what the 2026-09-21 pass added to them
is a *Diagnosis findings for this surface* table, which is a findings
list, not a plan. `07-messaging` says so itself: *"when this surface is
opened, each finding above should get its own slice id here so it can be
traced."*

**"The diagnosis has now been linked to each plan doc, right?"**
Verified by script rather than by reading: **51 findings in the
register, all 51 placed by the index, no ghosts, and every placement
real** — the doc the index names does carry that finding. 54 placements
for 51 findings, because D12, D17 and D48 sit in two docs each on
purpose. That half of the record is sound.

**The gap was the queue, not the findings.** Of 93 queued or parked
`BUILD_LIST` lines, **only 14 sat under a feature doc**; 76 sat in three
flat lists — the legacy check, carried from gamma, found during the port
— with no plan behind them. The 2026-09-21 pass gave every *finding* a
home; the *work* still had none. I had read that flat list to Sam at the
start of the session as though it were a plan, and it is not.

### The regroup (the work of the session)

Sam: *link the build list items to the docs too*. **All 87 marked lines
moved verbatim** under the surface that will build them; the three
lists are gone as groupings, their intros folded into the section intro
with the pointer to the legacy-check entry kept for the ~40 minor

against `HEAD`. Sections opened for 00, 01, 04, 06 and 07, which had

### The surface menu, for whoever picks next

Sam: *we will continue later*, surface by surface.

### What went wrong, and what it taught

- **I showed Sam a coverage table that was entirely false.** The
  checker built its regex by concatenation and the shell flattened
  `\b` into a literal backspace, so the pattern was `<BS>D4<BS>` and
  matched nothing; it reported every finding missing from every doc.
  I had read D4 in `01-payments.md` with my own eyes ten minutes
  earlier, and that contradiction is the only reason it was caught.
  **A check that disagrees with something already seen is a broken
  check until proven otherwise — say so and re-run before showing the
  output.** The rewrite avoids escaping entirely: collect every `D<n>`
  a doc mentions into a set and test membership.
- **A stale "what is open" list is not a fact.** See above: the release
  had happened and I repeated the entry instead of reading git, with
  the answer already on screen.
- `node` on Windows resolves a Git Bash `/tmp/x` path as `C:\tmp\x` and
  fails; scratch files take the session scratchpad's absolute path.
- Two files were rewritten by script rather than by hand precisely so
  the lines could not drift; the multiset diff is what made "moved
  verbatim" a claim rather than a hope.

### What is open

- **Nothing is unreleased.** Prod holds all 24 migrations; `production`
  contains `main`.
- **Unruled by Sam:** D4 (no Paystack webhook), D28 (six trips before a
  protected page), D50 (schools unmanageable), D3 (telegram keys free
  text); the four `window.prompt` boxes and the one `alert` in
  Announcements; the colliding D-series (`rebuild.md` D1–D10 against the
  diagnosis's D1–D51).
- The six placement calls above, any of which Sam may move.
- **91 of `BUILD_LIST`'s 170 lines are over the file's own 120-character
  cap** — the legacy-check block worst, one at 234 characters. Found
  today; every word left untouched by agreement, so it needs its own
  pass or a change to the rule.
- **`00-overview` is now the largest section at 20 lines.** If it keeps
  growing, the design system and cutover may each deserve a doc; noted,
  not decided.
- The five docs with no plan (00, 01, 04, 06, 07) are rewritten when
  their feature comes up — that rule is unchanged.

`AGENTS.md` gains the rule that every queued line sits under a surface
and that there are no flat lists, and its stale *"rewritten so far: 03,
05"* is corrected to 02, 03, 05, 08 with 09 written in the shape.
`BUILD_LIST.md`'s own rules block gains the same rule with Sam's two
placement rulings.

### B2 — the answers server-only (built, proven, walked, ticked)

`question_bank` still carried the schema's default `grant all` to
`anon` and `authenticated` on all 26 columns, RLS the only gate: a
student holding a course could read `correct`, the rationale and the
six feedbacks with their own credential (D8). Both builders pulled a
whole course's rows into the browser **including stems and
rationales**, only so the concept keyword could be matched locally
(D9) — ~540 rows of question text on RM_MID to run a substring filter.

**Sam's ruling (2026-09-21), asked before building:** the doc's B2
covered the reads; the write grants were not in it. Take them back
too. The bank's one write path is the admin page, so Save, Delete and
the CSV import move to the service role behind `requireAdmin()` and
the table is left with no browser write path at all.

`20260921120000_question_bank_secret_half.sql`: `revoke all` from both
browser roles, SELECT granted back to `authenticated` on the seventeen
public columns, `anon` holding nothing; the three admin write policies
dropped with the privilege they policed;
`search_question_bank_ids(course, query)` matching the wizard's four
fields — subtopic, main topic, stem, rationale — with `position()`.
**A function rather than a filter built in TypeScript because the
keyword is a student's own typing**: as a bound parameter it cannot
reach the query's shape, and `position()` has no LIKE wildcards to
escape, so it is the browser's `String.includes()` exactly. EXECUTE
off the browser roles.

Code: `lib/bank/queries.ts` split by client type — `ServiceDb` for the
whole row and the search, the cookie client for the filter options and
the id check — so a read needing the key cannot be handed a student's
client by accident; the compiler refuses it. `getItemsByIds` became
`knownItemIds()` (both callers used nothing but `item_id`).
`BuilderItem` lost `stem` and `rationale`, so no wizard code can
filter on question text. Both wizards call `searchBuilderConcepts()`
300 ms after the last keystroke, hold the previous result while one is
in flight so the pool does not blink, show "Searching…" and refuse
Build until it lands. The admin bank page and the quiz picker read and
write through the service role.

**One visible change, named to Sam before building:** the concept
keyword waits about a third of a second instead of filtering as you
type. The alternative — matching subtopic and main topic only, no
server call — was rejected: a student searching "cardiac" would stop
finding the questions where the word is only in the stem.

**Proven on dev** as student4: `select correct`, `select *` and
`update` all refused with *permission denied for table question_bank*;
the search function refused; 540 RM_MID stems still readable. The
search equals the old browser match exactly — `labour` gives 116 rows
both ways with no difference either direction, case-insensitive, an
empty query matching nothing rather than everything, and a typed `%`
matching the 7 rows that literally contain one rather than all 540.
**Walked:** the Quiz Builder's keyword (116 on screen, 116 in SQL),
the chips and the topic path (Labour & Delivery 114), a build into the
runner with a question answered and its feedback and rationale
arriving from the server; the offline builder (`breastfeeding`, 33 and
33) and a pack rendered. D9's proof was read off the wire: the course
load now sends six fields a row and the search reply bare ids. Admin:
540 loaded with the answers, a question created, edited (writing a
`rationale` — a column the browser role cannot even read) and deleted,

The Question Bank's Delete is `window.confirm()`, kept by the

### Sam's submission: group the findings by surface

Sam asked whether the diagnosis findings and the §8 rows — all things
we mean to do — are actually linked to the plans, "so we don't
build/improve things wrongly". Audited all 51 D findings and 14 S rows
against every doc and every BUILD_LIST line. **Four docs had done the
grouping (02, 03, 05, 08 — the four rewritten into the living-plan
shape); the other six claimed nothing, so 26 of the 51 findings had no
home** and could not be reached from any plan. Sam's instruction: group
them all that way, so we work surface by surface. Done as an
assignment pass, not a rewrite — a "Diagnosis findings for this
surface" section appended to 00, 01, 04, 06, 07 and 09, each finding
with its status; those docs still describe the legacy product above
that line and are rewritten when their feature comes up. **Sam's
ruling:** config, schools, levels and telegram keys have no surface, so
they fold into `00-overview.md` rather than opening a doc.
`post-rebuild-diagnosis.md` gains *Where each finding lives* as the
index.

Sam's own diagnosis of the root cause, recorded because it explains
half the register at once: legacy was a vanilla app, so **the browser
*was* the application** — it held the database credential and every
gate had to be a row-level policy. The port moved the code to the
server and carried the grants across untouched. That is D5, D7, D8,
D9, D25, D37, D43, D44, D46 and D49.

### D43, which had gone quiet

Found by the audit: **D43 — every table grants the browser roles
everything, RLS the only gate — was in no doc and on no BUILD_LIST
line.** It could not live in a feature doc because it belongs to all of
them, and that is exactly how it went quiet (found 2026-09-18, noted
in that entry as "last of the migrations", never queued). Sam: every
table will be touched anyway, so it is not a sweep — it becomes
**AGENTS.md rule 9: a slice that touches a table takes that table's
grants back**, not only a table it creates.

Measured on dev while deciding: of 27 tables, **6 are clean and 21
still hand the browser roles TRUNCATE**. The pattern is the reason for
the rule — the clean ones are almost all tables a slice *created*
(`question_bank`, `attempt_items`, `offline_pack_items`,
`course_access`, plus `attempts` from Q5); where a slice touched a
table that already existed it fixed the reads and left the writes:
**02 C1 left `subscriptions` and `product_courses` open, 03 Q1 left
`quizzes` and `mock_quizzes` open, S10 left `users` with TRUNCATE.**
Not a live hole — PostgREST exposes no TRUNCATE and RLS denies writes
with no policy — a weak floor, which is why Sam took the bank's grants
back today rather than trusting RLS alone.

### What went wrong, and what it taught

- **I reported the CSV import as proven when it had not happened.**
  Sam said "the csv import worked"; I wrote it up as the third admin
  write without checking the row count, and it was only when the
  arithmetic failed that he said he had not done it. A walk is proven
  by the database, not by the report of it.
- **I called a missing row before establishing what an unfamiliar row
  was.** `GP_360697` — the only row in 5,281 with no subject, topic or
  batch — I assumed came from that import, which made the bank look one
  row short; Sam was sent looking for a delete bug that did not exist.
  Its id shape (six digits, the New Question panel's) had already said
  it could not be the importer's, which stamps `Date.now()`. Follow the
  evidence to the end before raising an alarm.
- **The pane cannot answer `window.confirm()` and has no file picker**,
  so Delete and the CSV import both needed Sam. Named as a cost in the
  dialog rule; the CSV was handed over as a file in Downloads.
- Per-course id fingerprints (`md5(string_agg(item_id …))`) before and
  after a walk are cheap and settle these questions outright: GP 601 →
  604 on the import, back to 601 and the identical fingerprint after
  the three test rows were cleared, the other nine untouched throughout.

## Session — 2026-09-20 — **S7 built whole: the attempts restructure as 03 Q4 / Q5 / Q6 — the questions as rows, the answers as rows behind a service-role-only write door with grading in SQL, and the seal; MyNclex's attempt tables read as the reference and one row per question ruled; the runner-as-player captured; the cloud session's free account and gamification recorded as doc 09** — Claude

On `claude/work-session-49028b` in the desktop-app worktree
`work-session-135d16`, from `main` at `6d6119f`. Merged to `main` once
mid-session (`efd92e9`, after Q6; deploy-dev and migrate-dev green, the
dev migration run finding 0 pending) and asked for again at the end.
The dev server ran from the first action to the end; no background
readers — MyNclex was read through `gh api` into the scratchpad, by
hand. Sam signed the pane in as student4 for the walks.

### Where the session started

The prod release of 2026-09-19 evening had applied the ten migrations
the previous entry listed as pending (the run's log: 11 applied, 10
pending, all ten "done"), and Sam had made his two one-line checks; so
nothing stood before the next release. Sam declined announcements (A1)
and picked S7 from the list.

### S7 read against MyNclex, and the rulings

The §8 S7 row was ticked on 2026-09-18 but never sliced. Sam asked
whether `attempt_items` and `attempt_answers` (the diagnosis's draft)
could be one table, then asked for MyNclex's runner and attempt code to
be read. Read: its attempt tables (`slice_2_1`), the seven functions
(count and create, start, save progress, submit answer, complete,
expire, discard, the flag), the session page's `SEALED_ITEM_COLUMNS`,
the runner's clock and save-on-tap, and its later fixes. What it said
for us: its five modes collapse into three behaviours and two of them
are our two modes; every write is a locked function; save-on-tap
half a second after the tap; expire refuses before the deadline by the
server clock; finish inserts a skipped row per unanswered question (a
bug fix); discard keeps the answers (a reversal on 2026-09-01); the
seal is a column list at the page plus a type split, a per-item unseal
at submit, and a seeded unseal on resume; it seals at the page only and
accepts that a console query mid-exam reads the key. And on Sam's
question, its own record supports one table: it put a mutable flag on
its snapshot table, fused the two into one row for note-embedded
questions, and the skipped-row bug cannot exist with one row per
question from creation.

**Sam's rulings (2026-09-20), in 03's §3:** one row per question holding
the snapshot and the answer, no separate answers table; `started_utc`
and `ended_utc` on the header instead of overwriting `ts_iso` and using
`time_taken_s = 0` as the started flag; grading inside the database
function (three types; the browser never supplies a score); the secret
half revoked from the browser role, not only sealed at the page; every
function callable by the service role only; SATA all-or-nothing through
S7 (partial credit is Q7, its own conversation — Sam's first submission
answered: finish S7 first, the storage already holds marks and a
numeric score); discard keeps rows; retake copies fresh in the
original's order; tables → write door → seal (the diagnosis had seal
first). Sam's second submission — does the secret half really reach the
browser one at a time, and what about the console — answered as two
doors: the page seal closes the first, the column revoke the second.
The doc written first ("proceed with the doc"), Q4–Q6 with a done-when
each, Q7 under Later; §8 S7 carries the slicing note. Sam also asked
whether fixed quizzes reshuffle on retake: no — a retake copies the
origin's order (legacy's rule, so a mock cannot be re-rolled); the
options reshuffle per attempt.

### Q4 — the questions as rows (built, proven, walked, ticked)

`20260920120000_attempt_items.sql`: `attempt_items` (links, the public
half, the secret half, the analytics columns, the answer group for Q5;
`item_id` with no key to the bank on purpose), `offline_pack_items` the
same without the answer group, the two clocks on the header,
`offline_packs.item_ids` dropped; `revoke all` then SELECT to
`authenticated` on every column but the secret half (column-level, the
S10 shape), the read policy testing the owner as a set (the B1 shape),
the sequences revoked; `create_attempt()` and `create_offline_pack()`
copying the questions table to table from `question_bank` with
`unnest … with ordinality` (an unknown id dropped, none resolving
raises), EXECUTE off the browser roles. **Sam: no backfill, delete the
old attempts** — the migration deletes both tables' rows (dev's 21 and
3; prod's test sittings at the next release; D5 leaves them empty on
launch day anyway). Code: the four creators and the pack builder call
the functions through the service role after their gates; the loader
and `finishAttempt` read the rows with the service role after CHECK 4;
the retake takes its order from the origin's rows; the pack renderer
and the non-repeat picker read `offline_pack_items`; the "missing
count" apology gone; `offline_packs` added to `schema.sql` (it had been
missing). Proven by SQL, then walked as student4: builder, fixed quiz,
retake in the origin's order, a pack built and rendered, history and My
Packs; a bank stem edited after creation left both copies untouched.

### Q5 — the answers as rows and the write door (built, proven, walked, ticked)

`20260920150000_attempt_answers.sql`: `grade_answer()` (MCQ / TF by
letter, SATA the exact set, empty = wrong) and six doors, each taking
the caller's user id and refusing a row not theirs or an attempt not in
progress — `start_timed_attempt` (once; the second tab loses),
`save_answers` (a jsonb patch per question, keys optional, never the
graded columns), `check_answer` (writes and grades one row, returns its
secret half), `finish_attempt` (every row graded from its final answer
— instant mode lets a student change an answer after the reveal, as
legacy did; the header summed; an exam's time from the server clock),
`expire_attempt` (refuses before the deadline; closes at the deadline
with the full length), `abandon_attempt` (rows kept); the walk's rows
carried over from `answers_json`, the two blobs dropped, the student
INSERT / UPDATE policies dropped with the default grants taken back,
CHECKs on `status` / `mode` / `source`. Code: the actions a gate and one
call each; the runner saves one patch per question half a second after
the last tap, a flag and a page turn at once (**legacy gap 1 closed**),
instant mode's pick / Check Answer calls the function, a failed Submit
shows a toast and unlocks (**gap 2 closed**), the timed auto-submit
retries every 10 s; the loader expires an overdue exam on open; the
quiz cards' "N of M answered" and the admin detail count rows;
`autosaveMs` gone from the pages (the config key stays for S13).
Proven: select-only on `attempts`, a direct UPDATE of `score_pct` and a
direct INSERT refused, none of nine functions executable by the browser
roles, the SATA cases. Walked as student4: per-tap saves and a flag
read back within a second, a reload restoring 3 / 10 and the flag,
finished 4 / 10; an exam's start pushed back 11 min by SQL and the next
open closing it at the deadline (600 s, the 8 unanswered graded wrong),
landing in review at 1 / 10; a practice attempt abandoned under an open
runner, Submit toasting with the runner unlocked and no score card.

**One defect found and fixed in the walk:** the expiry fired but the
page still showed the preflight — Next memoises an identical fetch
within one render, so the loader's second read of the attempt through
the cookie client returned the stale row. The re-read goes through the
service role (a distinct request). A workaround line in AGENTS.md.

### Q6 — the seal (built, proven, walked, ticked)

No migration. `lib/attempts/seal.ts` is the one place a row is cut:
`sealItem()` the public half and the answer group, `secretOf()` the
key, the rationale and the per-option feedback, both column lists
explicit. Types: `SealedItem` (no `correct`, so runner code cannot read
the key off a question), `SecretHalf`, `AttemptItem` the two together
server-side only, `SecretsMap`. The loader seals every row and fills
the map by the rule — nothing for a live exam, the rows already graded
for a live instant attempt, every row in review and admin preview. The
runner holds the map as state: Check Answer's reply adds one question's
secret half, the finish and expire replies add every question's (so
the review renders without a reload); the feedback, the correct marks
and the rationale render from the map; the reveal waits for the secret;
the grid's colouring takes the server's `is_correct` first; the score
is the finish reply's or the header's — the browser's own arithmetic is
gone. **Proven as student4 (the D5 check both ways):** a live exam's
page, fetched with the session, holds all 10 stems and 0 `correct`, 0
`rationale`, 0 `fb_a`, the map empty; the same exam closed opens in
review with 10 keys and the rationales in the page; a practice attempt
after two picks reloads with exactly 2 keys, question 1 showing its
feedback and question 3 none; submitted at 3 / 10, a question never
checked before submit shows its feedback from the finish reply.

**Noticed:** the first Check Answer on a cold Worker took 1.7 s — before
Q6 the feedback was instant because the browser held the key. A pending
state on the option is Q9 (captured, not ruled).

### The runner as a player (captured, not ruled)

Sam: MyNclex's runner is a player — it reads the header and behaves,
whatever the source. Ours already is, mostly: it obeys the mode, the
duration, the label, the status and the rows, and never reads the
source. Three shortfalls: the mode is in the URL as well as the row
(two routes, legacy's two pages; CHECK 6 bounces), Exit always goes to
Fixed Quizzes, the words are keyed by mode only. Captured in 03 under
Later with MyNclex's runner as reference (one route, exit per source,
intent and five modes in three behaviours, per-type components, the
mobile layout, the tutorial; "session" / "sitting" on screen, attempt
in storage), Q8 the runner as a player and Q9 the pending state as
candidates, and Q3's header instructions (results withheld until a
date, no retake, one sitting in a window, a sequential mode). Sam: B2
first, then the runner; the conversation when it comes up. Legacy gaps
1 and 2 ticked on BUILD_LIST as closed by Q5.

### The cloud session, read and recorded

Sam had held a cloud session on 2026-09-20 ("Passwell Consult source
inspection") and asked for it to be read; it wrote nothing to the repo

### What went wrong, and what it taught

- **Next's request memoisation** (above): a second identical read in the
  same render after a write returns the first read's result. Re-read
  through a different client or after the response.
- The first Check Answer after a quiet spell is a visible wait now that
  the browser has no key. Expected; Q9 is the answer.
- The dev-server log filled with "export does not exist" notices in the
  seconds between editing a caller and its module; they look like
  errors after the fact. Harmless; read the timestamps.
- Ticking a BUILD_LIST line with its date pushed four lines past the
  120 cap; each was trimmed in its own small commit. Count after every
  tick.

- **Prod, at the next release:** two migrations (`attempt_items`,
  `attempt_answers`); the first deletes prod's test attempts and packs
  (Sam's ruling). Nothing else pending.
- **08 B2** next (Sam): the bank's secret half off the browser roles
  and the builders' search server-side — the one bank read left on the
  student side; unblocked by S7. Then a release with all three.
- **03:** Q7 partial credit (a rule, then the three-state display); Q8
  the runner as a player and Q9 the pending state, ruled after B2; Q3's
  design; `quiz_items`; the analytics by SQL over `attempt_items`.
- **09:** every question in its §4 before any slice; the NMC Prep walk
  in the pane on Sam's account first (the daily challenge, the
  leaderboard, the past-papers claim); the pool's door is a §8 row.
- **The design system:** the shell and teal mockups, then Radix or the
  current structure, then the doc.
- Dev data: student4 holds today's walk sittings (four completed, one
  abandoned, two packs); student1 the Q4 proof attempt and pack.

### What went wrong, and what it taught

- **Two rules fell to Sam's walks within the hour they were built** —
  both because I reasoned from the case in front of me (edit the newer
  receipt; revoke the later one) and not its mirror (edit the older;
  revoke the earlier). A rule about an ordered set has to be stated for
  every position in the order before it is built. The chain is the
  version that has no position-dependent case.
- **`revoke insert, update, delete` on a new table is not "no browser
  write path"** — TRUNCATE, REFERENCES, TRIGGER stay under the schema's
  default grant. `revoke all` then the one grant. (Also below.)
- **The dev server hot-reloads the readers before the migration runs.**
  In C1 the code was edited first and the migration applied minutes
  later; Sam's dashboard loads in between logged "no relationship
  between products and product_courses". For C2 the migration was
  applied the moment the readers were written. Habit: migrate first,
  or write and migrate in one breath. In memory.
- **`revoke insert, update, delete` is not "no browser write path"** on
  this schema: the default privileges grant all, so TRUNCATE stays
  unless `revoke all` is used. A new table gets `revoke all` then the
  one grant it needs.
- A `coalesce(' revoked', '')` in a proof query printed REVOKED on every
  live row — a query bug, caught by the function's own result. Proof
  queries read the column, not a literal.

### What is open

- **Prod, before the next release:** Sam's two one-line checks (the
  duplicate-email index; every product course word has a `courses`
  row); **ten migrations pending on prod** (auth floor, payments scrub,
  quiz floor, product_courses, course_access, its grants, created_utc,
  requested_start, question_bank). The `question_bank` file counts
  before it drops; prod's ids are its own.
- **02 is built through C3b.** Left under it: per-course editing if a
  real case asks; **gap 11** (an archived course inside a product)
  still Sam's ruling; the Sync button's future; the Users dashboard's

- **08:** B2 (the answers server-only) after S7; B3 (the admin page

- **Next:** A1 / A2, S13 / S11 / the payments group / S7 — Sam's

`work-session-135d16`, from `main` at `d5ee034`. Fourteen commits,
merged to `main` three times during the session (27bf36d after S9 +

whether Sam said yes). Every push deployed `licensure-dev` green and

### The design-system inventory (no server, no readers)

This repo's `styles/` read directly; MyNclex's through `gh api` and a
tarball of its `styles/`, `docs/`, `components/shell/` into the
scratchpad. **The palette is already one:** MyNclex's `tokens.css`
says in its header that its palette was copied from MyTeacher and
MyNMCLicensure — the twelve core colours, the 8px radius and the
shadows are identical; MyNclex has six extra tokens. The differences
that matter: MyNclex's token file also defines `.btn`, `.form-group`
and `.alert` once (5 of 59 files override `.btn`), while this app's
`base.css` holds tokens and the toast only and **20 of 32 stylesheets
define their own button** (legacy's per-page `<style>` blocks,
transcribed); this app has **two teals** (the app's `#2d7d72`; the
sales pages' `#0b7a75` from legacy's premium-prep / subscribe /
payment-confirmation, carried into Upgrade and the pack renderer); the
shells differ (dark sidebar here, white topbar + light sidebar + footer
+ student bottom tabs there); neither app has a named type or radius
scale (30 font sizes and ~20 radii here); no shared confirm dialog in
either (9 hand-built here); MyNclex's Tailwind is installed and unused.
Recommendation given: the foundation is MyNclex's token file extended
with status tokens, copied here as `styles/tokens.css`; the shell stays
per product; the second teal is Sam's call; per-page buttons collapse
as each page is touched. Sam wanted to *see* it rather than read names,
so a Claude Design prompt was written with the real values, the real
menu labels and the two open decisions (A1/A2 shell, B1/B2 teal) as
side-by-side screens; the mockups are pending, the doc waits on them.
One BUILD_LIST line.

### S9 + S10 — the auth floor (built, walked, merged)

`20260919120000_auth_floor.sql` plus seven code files. The five
login / reset functions revoked from `public`, `anon`, `authenticated`
(the service role keeps its grant from the schema migration; a new
parameter is a new signature, so the old ones are dropped first and
each REVOKE follows its CREATE because the schema's default privileges
grant every new routine to the browser roles); `auth_events.ip_hash`
and the limiter counting by IP as a third key — **20 per 10 min and 50
per 24 h, wider than email and fingerprint's 5 and 10** because a
campus router or a mobile carrier puts many students on one address
(my judgment call, flagged to Sam, his to change); the two limit
checks fail closed with logging fail-open (D30, `RateLimitCheck` with
three states); `username` and `must_change_password` dropped;
`users_insert` dropped and INSERT revoked, UPDATE revoked and granted
back on the nine profile-page columns, the profile insert and
Deactivate through the service role; email lowercased by a trigger and
unique on `lower(email)`. **Proven on dev:** the five answer 401 to
the anon key; a student cannot execute them, insert a row, or update
email / program_id, and can update forename / cohort; a mixed-case
email stores lowercase. **Walked by Sam:** wrong password refused and
logged with the IP, sign-in, both profile saves, a capitals-email
registration stored lowercase with the welcome mail sent, Deactivate
then Reactivate through the server. The two test accounts (the
capitals one and slice2a-test) deleted from dev by explicit-id
statements. One thing seen in the walk, logged as a BUILD_LIST line:
**a deactivated account's sign-in is logged LOGIN_SUCCESS and the gate
throws it out too fast to read anything** — refuse at login with a
message, log a refusal. **Prod:** the migration's unique index fails
if two prod accounts share an address in different casing; Sam runs
the one-line duplicate check before the next release.

### D32 + D36 — the Paystack trim (built, proven, merged)

`lib/payments/trim.ts` allow-lists the verify reply (id, reference,
status, amount, currency, channel, paid_at, gateway_response,
customer.email, authorization.{channel, card_type, last4}) and the
initialize reply (reference); verify's two raw writes and both init
steps use it; the currency compared beside the amount, a mismatch
FAILED with a note and `currency_mismatch` named on the confirmation
page; `20260919150000_payments_raw_scrub.sql` trims the stored rows
with a `pg_temp` helper (idempotent; dev holds zero payment rows, prod
at the next release). **Proven** with a full Paystack-shaped reply:
none of nineteen sensitive tokens survive; the SQL scrub over the
same shape on dev matches. No browser walk possible (the pane cannot
finish a checkout); the currency branch was read, not run.

### The record: the feature docs become the living plans

Sam's question — do the D findings sit inside plan docs? — and the
follow-up — do we not have existing plan docs? Yes: `00–07`, the
legacy descriptions, ruled "not build plans" on 2026-09-12. **Ruled
2026-09-19:** they become the living plan per feature, MyNclex's shape
— what it does today, what the diagnosis found, Sam's rulings, the
sliced plan with the doc's own ids and a done-when per slice, a
ladder — rewritten one at a time when the feature comes up (Sam: it
shows what existed first and what has now changed); BUILD_LIST gets a

### Q1 + Q2 — the quiz floor and the lifecycle rules (built, walked, merged)

**Q1** `20260919170000_quiz_floor.sql`: the two SELECT policies become
admin or an active published row of a course the caller holds
(`user_has_course`); `item_ids` and `notes` off the browser roles'
reach, column-level — which means the admin's own cookie session too,
so the attempt spawn, the admin edit step and the whole-table admin
reads take the service role behind their gates; the student reads name
the card's columns (`QuizCard`); CHECKs on the status words, `n`
defaulted on mocks. **Proven on dev** as the RN student: three of five
quizzes and both mocks (the two RM quizzes gone), `item_ids` and
`notes` refused; the admin reads all five; a bogus status refused.
**Q2**, code only: `startRefusal()` shared by Start and Retake on the
server's clock, Retake following a retake chain back to its quiz; the
list pages pass the server's render time; archive one-way with restore
landing on draft (`setQuizStatus` accepts two words); `saveQuiz`
validates; the stats box keyed by table and id with first sittings,
retakes and abandons apart; the mock admin list paged through the one
kind-aware paginated read. **Walked by Sam:** both student lists,
Start, the course previews, admin Edit with its questions, the paged
mock list, the six-figure stats box, archive → restore to draft, Retake
refused on a closed quiz. **D45 (h) ruled kept** (Sam, 2026-09-19):
Edit would otherwise open empty.

### Two rulings on the way

- **Course ids stay codes** (Sam asked whether the alpha-era ids
  `GP`, `RN_MED`, born for Blogger page URLs, should give way to
  generated ids). The Blogger reason is gone; the code is quoted in
  support, the importer, announcements and every SQL check, and
  programmes and products follow the same convention. The real cost
  of a new course is the per-course item table (S2), not the id.
  Recommended and accepted: the form suggests a code from programme +
  title, overwritable; drop the dead `page_slug` when the table is
  next touched; **S2 before S8**. One BUILD_LIST line.
- **S8 read back** at Sam's request (the products → courses link
  table and the course_access rows, his framing "GP for 365 days, not
  RN Full for 365 days"): first step when picked is rewriting
  `02-subscriptions.md` into the living plan, then two or three slices.

### What went wrong, and what it taught

- The dev RLS test pattern had a trap: `set local role authenticated`
  BEFORE `set_config` with a subquery on an RLS table returns null
  claims (the subquery sees no rows) and a JSON error that looks like
  a policy failure. `set_config` first, then the role. In memory.
- The anon-key probe returns 404, not 401, when the body carries an
  argument the function does not have — the "before" probe read as
  "already refused" until the bodies were fixed.
- The Q1 revoke bit the admin too (the admin's cookie client is
  `authenticated`); caught while writing the slice, not after —
  written into the doc so A1's function grants stay right.

- **Prod, before the next release:** the duplicate-email check (one
  line, Sam runs it); three migrations pending on prod (auth floor,
  payments scrub, quiz floor).
- **A1 and A2** of `05-announcements.md`; then S13 / S11 / the
  payments group / S7, S8 (02 doc first, S2 before S8) — the order
  stands as given, Sam's to change.
- **The design-system doc** after Claude Design's mockups: the A1/A2
  shell and B1/B2 teal decisions, then `styles/tokens.css`.
- D28, D50 unruled; D45 (g) waits on the mock premium design.
- The prod Paystack scrub runs itself at the next release.

## Session — 2026-09-18/19 — **Sam's rulings on D31–D36 and the general limiter; three table sweeps traced alpha → gamma → port — messaging (D37–D43), quizzes / mock exams / announcements (D44–D48), config / schools / levels (D49–D51) — with S11, S12 and S13 ticked; the table-by-table sweep declared complete; a design system opened; no code changed** — Claude

On `claude/work-session-74e97e` in the desktop-app worktree
`work-session-90f627`, from `main` at `bf90fd3`. Fourteen docs

### The payments rulings (D31–D36), one at a time

- **D31 — pay-first stays.** A product decision, not a Worker side
  effect (Sam: a buyer who has paid finishes the form; an account form
  first loses customers). Account-first rejected. Under pay-first the
  server creates the account the moment verify sees PAID, from the
  email, phone and programme already on the row, activates on that
  user id and queues a set-password link; the confirmation page's form
  shrinks to a password; the setup token, its two columns and the
  admin rescue go. Alpha's Payments app **was** webhook-driven (Sam) —
  noted on D4, which the D34 sweep now covers within a day.
- **D32 — strip, keep three.** Channel, card type, last four; drop the
  rest of `authorization` and `ip_address`; scrub existing rows.
- **D33 — same browser, emailed link as fallback.** A private cookie
  set at init proves the browser; without it, status plus "we emailed
  you a link". Emailed-link-only rejected (it sends every buyer to the
  inbox at the moment they are readiest).
- **D34 — a nightly sweep that asks Paystack.** Sam's question first:
  what were the statuses for? A rescue checklist (doc 01); as a
  "started but did not finish" signal INIT is not done right. Stale
  INIT rows verified with Paystack: paid → activation, else ABANDONED,
  never deleted; SETUP_REQUIRED goes.
- **D35 — one tally per action, verify per reference, fail closed.**
  Explained twice; the second time as a bouncer counting a building.
  Sam's follow-up: the `rate_limits` table was built in 9a from
  §7.1 of the plan he approved 2026-09-10, not asked again at build.
- **D36 — currency checked**, a rider on D32.
- **The general limiter queued** (Sam: one tally sheet, one method,
  a rule per door; a bouncer says "not that often", never "not at
  all"). First doors as examples only: register, messages, both
  builders, photo upload; shaped at build.
- BUILD_LIST: five payments lines, the limiter, three ✖ (§9 #20, #21,
  the §9 #4 rollback).

### Sweep: messaging → D37–D43, S11

Traced: alpha-native (two sheet tabs behind a token-checked Apps
Script API with a 2000-char cap, send and bulk rate limits, a
200-recipient cap, paged lists); gamma moved the boundary to the
browser on 2026-03-20, dropped all five, ran twelve days without RLS,
and its thread INSERT policy lacked the admin bypass so admin threads
**never worked live until June**; the port put writes behind the gates
as the user's client and batched Bulk Send. **Proven on dev** (SQL as a
student, rolled back): a forged admin reply in one's own thread,
edited admin replies, the thread's status and admin rewritten;
isolation between students holds; the code reader's "a thread can be
re-owned" was **false** (USING applies to the new row when WITH CHECK
is absent) — recorded as method: **test a policy claim before recording
it.** D43 found along the way: `grant all` to `anon` and
`authenticated` on all 32 tables; RLS is the only gate (schema-wide,
its own migration after the sweeps). Sam's rulings **on the feature,
not finding by finding**: keep the support desk; **park Bulk Send**
("keep but rarely use" weighed and rejected — a rarely used door is
the one nobody watches); **split question feedback into a reports
feature**. Then six decisions as recommended, drafted and ticked as
**S11**: browser writes revoked, service-role writes, messages fixed
once sent, 2000-char cap, visible reopen, `student_read_at` /
`admin_read_at` replacing the flags, the course link opens a draft,
`admin_id` and the five bulk / question columns dropped.

Traced: fixed quizzes are alpha's sheet (no item list; twin rows per

counts; D48 residue. **Sam's intention on mocks:** for premium members,
an exam-period engagement, meant as a different experience — the
record agrees three times; the merge into one table (my proposal)
**withdrawn**; `visibility` kept as the gate's flag; "Mock exams as a
premium exam experience" a design item, ingredients noted, none
decided. Four rulings as recommended → **S12** ticked: course-scoped
SELECT with `item_ids` / `notes` server-only, `announcements_for_me()`
with the table admin-only, three notice timestamps with a key, the D45

D51: `levels` unread, level and cohort free text in three places. Sam's

Every table in `licensure_gh` traced once and judged; D43's grant

### A design system, opened

Sam asked what a design system is, whether one is needed, and whether
per app or across Quademia. Answered: one shared foundation (named
values, common components, the AGENTS.md rules), a product layer per
app, shared by copy under rule #2; the first decision is **whose look
is the source** (MyNclex's, this app's legacy look, or new). Sam asked
for an inventory of both apps' current foundations; two readers were
launched (this repo's `styles/`; MyNclex via `gh api`) and **did not
finish** — the machine crashed twice while they and the dev server
ran. Nothing recorded from them; rerun next session, one at a time,
without the dev server.

### What went wrong, and what it taught

- A reader's confident policy claim was false; a 30-second SQL test as
  the student settled it. Test before recording.
- My own SQL used a status word that did not exist (`published` for
  `active`) and produced a plausible wrong count. Read the live values
  before counting them.
- The auto-mode classifier refused a mass DELETE inside a transaction
  I would roll back; the no-DELETE-policy fact was recorded as read,
  not proven.
- Two background readers plus the dev server twice crashed Sam's
  machine. Stopped agents resume with `SendMessage` to their id and
  keep their progress — but not across a crash mid-flight. **Next
  session: no parallel readers while the dev server runs**; one at a
  time, or the inventory done by hand.

### The port is finished

Sam: "why did we call the rebuild rebuild — we didn't rebuild, we
changed the stack." Agreed: it was a port, and the name had needed two
rules (D4, the ⭐ rule) to stop people reading it the natural way.
Sam's ruling: the port ended 2026-09-16; everything since is
improvement of one app; cutover is one item on the list, not a wall
the list is sorted around; nothing has to wait for real users; new
features are allowed, each still Sam's go-ahead; `legacy/` is a
reference now, not the spec. **Record changed:** AGENTS.md Current
Status and the ⭐ rule rewritten; BUILD_LIST's "The rebuild" section
retitled the port, one *Improvements* list holding the legacy-check
gaps, Carried from gamma and *Found during the port and since*; the
diagnosis opening no longer leans on the ⭐ rule; rebuild.md's header
says it is finished work kept as history with §8 still live.
`rebuild.md` otherwise untouched. On the question "are S7 and S8 new
features?" — improvements, except S8's queued-start rule and D23's
ordering, which are new behaviour; the label decides nothing now.

### The auth group (users, sessions, auth_events, reset_requests, rate_limits)

Sam's brief: investigate the *intention* of the auth surfaces before
improving them — "this is how hackers could get into the app" — and
trace them from the alpha era, where admin pages existed that gamma
never built. Done in three passes, all in `post-rebuild-diagnosis.md`:

1. **The sweep.** Every caller read; three things proven on dev: the
   five login / reset functions answer to the public anon key over
   plain HTTP (200 `{"allowed": true}`; the revoked payments one 401);
   a student's own JWT can rewrite `email`, `program_id`,
   `signup_source`, `must_change_password`, `last_login_utc` (role
   refused); and the chain from a rewritten email to the pay-first
   payer lookup, which activates a victim's paid subscription on the
   attacker's account. → **D24–D30** (grants; the writable row; the
   email copy stored as typed while Auth lowercases; registration
   depending on "Confirm email" being off; six trips per page;
   write-only audit tables with `last_login_utc` never written; the
   limits failing open), plus *What came out clean*.
2. **The trace.** The alpha auth scripts and sheet CSVs are in git at
   `40f2930^` under `old stack references for claude/` (deleted
   2026-04-12). Alpha owned passwords, tokens and reset tokens, had a
   MODERATOR role, and shipped four admin pages: Create User, Token
   Audit (with Revoke), Auth & Reset Audit (two tabs, filters), Expiry
   Reminders (a button over an Apps Script time trigger — Sam: the
   real cron lived in the script settings). Gamma recreated the tables
   "for audit trail and rate limiting" and deferred every page (the
   gamma BUILD_LIST at `70f686e`: "Admin UI deferred"; "last_login_utc
   — wire up or drop"). Gamma's first login page redirected a
   must-change user to a page never written; the 2026-04-07 fix removed
   the redirect. So the "dead" columns are the residue of four deferred
   admin pages. Recorded as a table, alpha → gamma → port, per column.
3. **The read-back.** Nine intentions taken one at a time, each with
   what it is, where it came from, done or not, options. Sam's rulings
   (in the diagnosis under the trace): keep the cap and fix gap 6;
   revoke + count by IP; fail closed + gap 8 legacy's way; the sessions
   view *fully* (drawer panel and platform page — "we do it fully if we
   are doing it"); the login-events tab; the reset tab *with actions*
   (Send reset link, Lift the block, admin sends logged — the alpha
   page was read-only); **Create User not rebuilt** — *Invite by email*
   (student or admin, optional product, a set-password link) replaces
   it and `username` + `must_change_password` go, because a user
   without a subscription is just a trial student and the real gap is
   acting on someone with no account, which the pay-first setup flow
   already solves in shape; last login written and shown; expiry
   reminders on a daily pg_cron doorbell (MyNclex's
   `email_drain_cron.sql` + `app/cron/*/route.ts` + `CRON_SECRET`), no
   run-now button. Then, from the email review, **item 10**: each app
   on the shared project takes over its Supabase-sent emails by minting
   links with `generateLink`, through an email outbox with a drain
   copied from MyNclex, built before the reminders.
   **§8 S9 ticked** (the revokes, IP in the limiter, the two columns
   dropped) and **S10 ticked** (column-level revoke on `users`, the
   service-role profile insert and admin Deactivate, `email` kept as a
   lowercased unique copy — reading from Auth rejected, ~15 readers).
   BUILD_LIST: nine lines under Improvements, the two parked gamma
   lines replaced, gaps 6 and 8 annotated.

### The payments group (payments, rate_limits)

Traced and recorded, rulings deferred to next session. Alpha was
**account-first** (`apiSelfRegisterPaid`, then a server-to-server
`payments_activate` with a shared secret, idempotent on the reference
with a pipe-list, a lock); gamma inverted it to **pay-first** on
2026-03-17 and never had a webhook (the April log's "webhook" was the
browser return). → **D31–D36**: pay-first as the root of §9 #20–#22,
D25's chain and the setup token; `payments.raw` keeping Paystack's
whole reply — `last4`, `bin`, `card_type`, `bank`, `ip_address` and a
*reusable* `authorization_code` (confirmed against `PaystackHQ/openapi`
`dist/paystack.yaml` and the docs page in the pane) — shown to admins
by the raw toggle; the reference alone returning the payer's email,
phone, programme and setup token; abandoned INIT rows never cleared and
creatable by anyone; one limiter bucket per address for four routes;
currency unchecked. Two questions for Sam next time: was the alpha
Payments web app webhook-driven or browser-driven, and was gamma's
pay-first a product choice or a Worker side effect.

### What went wrong, and what it taught

- The auth findings were first reported as seven holes; Sam redirected
  to *intention first*. The trace changed the fix for the dead columns
  from "drop or keep" to "rebuild the four admin pages or not", and
  turned Create User into Invite. **Trace the origin before judging a
  table** — now in the diagnosis's sweeps note as the method.
- A `for f in $(git ls-tree …)` loop over paths with spaces sprayed
  `fatal:` lines but still surfaced the alpha admin page names. Quote
  paths; or list once and read by exact path.
- The Paystack docs page renders client-side (`curl` finds nothing);
  the OpenAPI spec on GitHub and `javascript_tool` over the page in the
  pane both worked. `gh api search/code` missed MyNclex's webhook
  handler; its tree listing shows only `lib/payments/paystack.ts`, so
  where MyNclex verifies the webhook signature was not confirmed.
- `head -c` on a `curl -o /dev/stdout` hid the body; `-w` after the
  body with no pipe showed it.

On `claude/git-pull-setup-409342` in the desktop-app worktree
`work-session-135d16`, from `main` at `243d7ad`; `main` moved to

session tired; the merge to `main` was asked for at the end — git
records the answer.

### Housekeeping first

`origin/claude/busy-cannon-p7tcb7` (the 2026-09-17 cloud session, docs
only, five commits) was fast-forwarded into `main` and pushed on Sam's
word. **Its remote branch is still there**: the auto-mode classifier
refused `git push origin --delete` as destructive. Sam runs the delete
himself. `git branch --no-merged main` was otherwise empty.

### Why

Sam: the rebuild copied the browser-era shapes, so the server actions
and the data get redesigned so the database, not the page, is the gate.
Start with subscriptions — "most of the ideas are from the Sheets +
Blogger era when we had to use course ids to determine the pages to
load." One correction on the way in: the access *decision* already runs
on the server; what stayed browser-era is the *shape* and the database's
view of the browser's own credential (D10).

### What was found (D13–D21, all in `post-rebuild-diagnosis.md`)

Access has two definitions (TypeScript sums days from
`products.courses_included`, SQL checks each row's expiry) that agree by
coincidence · the days shown are a **sum nothing grants** whenever two
products overlap, and the sum drifts earlier every day (Sam's example:
RN Full then RM Full on day 65 shows GP 665, gets 365) · the access read
runs four or five times a request · the product→course link is text with
no FK · announcements and offline packs each pick "the" subscription by
their own rule · quizzes are not course-gated in SQL · Telegram keys go
to signed-out visitors · EXPIRED is a manual button · and the dev proof,
as two students' own JWTs, that the bank gate holds (0 rows from a
course not held, 900 with answers from one held) while the browser
roles hold table-wide grants, so a column-level REVOKE is available.

### What was decided (Sam, 2026-09-17/18)

- **Expiry belongs on the course, not the product.** Sam's own framing,
  after the RN Full + RM Full walk-through: a purchase writes one access
  row per course; the subscription stays as the receipt. Two tables
  designed: `product_courses` (definition, FKs) and `course_access`
  (user, course, subscription, start, expires, `revoked_utc`). One SQL
  function replaces both readers.
- **Six rulings** on it, recorded under *Proposed direction —
  course-level access*: bought means kept; a trial does not stack; the
  tables and the four automatic paths before cutover with "starts
  today", queued-row stacking on after cutover; revoke and edits do not
  ripple; every row traces to a receipt (product nullable for a
  hand-picked grant); the admin forms follow the rows (read-only before
  cutover, editing after).
- **S7 and S8 ticked into `rebuild.md` §8** (2026-09-18); S3 marked
  superseded by S7. Sam: "we are not building yet, we are diagnosing and
  restructuring" — so no BUILD_LIST lines, no order, no stage chosen.
- **S7's tables designed** with MyNclex's three attempt tables read from
  GitHub (`gh api … --jq .content | base64 -d`; the repo is not on this
  machine): `attempts` header minus the two blobs; `attempt_items` the
  snapshot with the sealed / unsealed column lists and **no FK on
  `item_id`** on purpose; `attempt_answers` separate because a snapshot
  is written once and an answer changes on every click;
  `offline_pack_items` the same machinery. One place stricter than
  MyNclex: the secret-half columns revoked from the browser role on
  `attempt_items`, so a timed mock is an exam even against the console.
- Sam's earlier "we will add to the diagnosis findings" and "not
  BUILD_LIST" held throughout.

### The dead-column sweep (read out, not recorded)

All 23 tables, each column grepped for a reader and a writer. Going with
S7/S8 anyway: `attempts.item_ids`, `answers_json`, `seed`,
`offline_packs.item_ids`, `products.courses_included`. No reader or no
writer today: `users.username`, `last_login_utc`, `must_change_password`,
`signup_source`; `subscriptions.expiry_reminded`;
`mock_quizzes.visibility`; `courses.page_slug`; `sessions.kind`,
`login_via`, `last_seen_utc`; `schools.ownership`, `programmes`;
`user_notice_state.seen_at`; the `levels` table; the config row
`builder_default_questions`. One smell: every student-opened thread is
inserted with `admin_id = 'admin1'` (`lib/messaging/actions.ts:79`).
**Sam: "some of them have a purpose, I just never really built them" —
to be looked at one at a time later.** Nothing written to the diagnosis
for this; a ⬜ line in BUILD_LIST so it is not lost.

### What went wrong, and what it taught

- The remote-branch delete is refused by the classifier; the AGENTS.md
  step "delete the remote branch after its merge" is Sam's to run.
- A `grep -c $'\r$'` inside `$( )` in one Bash call reported 0 CRLF on a
  file that `od` showed as CRLF; the Edit tool did keep CRLF. Check line
  endings with `od -c | head`, not a quoted grep in a substitution.
- I proposed the link table first and Sam asked whether it was "the same
  column made a table" — it was, on its own; the single access function
  and the per-course rows were the real fix. Say the important change
  first.

### After the merge (same session, Sam's questions)

`courses.program_scope` explained (which programmes sit a course — the
syllabus, kept; not recorded, at Sam's request). Then the eleven item
tables: Sam asked whether merging them was considered (S2, unticked,
"keep") and whether one table survives growth to more programmes. It
does — the per-table shape is what makes the twelfth course a deploy —
so **D22** was recorded and S2's recommendation in §8 reversed, decision
cell unchanged. Committed after the merge; asked for a second merge.

Then the sales pages, read against S8: three doors each define "for
sale" (D2), none adapts to the programme, Subscribe's programme pick
changes nothing, the Upgrade page ignores the student's own. I proposed
`products.program_id`; Sam rejected it (a product is a bag of courses,
a bag can cross programmes) and reframed the goal — anyone buys any
product, the page *orders* by programme, hides nothing. The match rule
is derived from `program_scope`, GP not counting (my first "every
course" version was backwards — Sam: a mixed RM + RN product should show
to both). Then D1: Sam proposed `products.is_premium` instead of
`programs.prep_product_id` — the year leaves the id, the programme comes
from the derived match, two premium RN products show as two cards; the
trial pointer stays because registration must pick exactly one. Recorded
as D1 option D (C replaced, with the reason) and **D23**. `program_scope`
→ `course_programs` when D23 is built.

Last, course-level pricing (a standalone price and duration on each
course, products only as bundles, the eleven `_ONLY` products retired,
a basket later). Weighed: a tidier catalogue and a visible bundle saving
against a second unit of sale through payments, receipts, emails and
every sales list. **Sam: keep the product model** — the single unit of
sale — and revisit course pricing when the course count makes a product
per course a chore; S8's shapes (one access row per course, a receipt
with no product) already fit it. Parked in BUILD_LIST, not a finding.

### What is open

- S7 and S8 are approved shapes with no order, stage or BUILD_LIST line
  — Sam's call when building starts. S2 (one item table, D22) is the
  third shape awaiting a tick.
- The dead-column list above, one at a time, with Sam saying which have
  an unbuilt purpose.
- D15 (per-request cache) and D19 (column selection on the sales pages)
  are code-only and independent of both rows.
- The table-by-table sweep has covered subscriptions and its neighbours
  only; the page-by-page sweep is unrun.
- Everything the 2026-09-16 night entry left open (the fourteen legacy
  gaps, 1–4 first).
- `origin/claude/busy-cannon-p7tcb7` to delete (Sam).

## Session — 2026-09-17 — **post-rebuild diagnosis: a new findings register opened, twelve structural findings recorded, the attempts restructure proposed as a draft §8 row (S7); MyNclex read for comparison; no code changed, nothing approved** — Claude

On `claude/busy-cannon-p7tcb7`, a cloud session, so **the branch is on
the remote** — a fact, not a plan: the container is ephemeral and
discarded work is lost. Three docs commits on it. **No code changed;
nothing merged.** Sam went off before a merge could be asked for, so
`main` is untouched. `npm run lint:check` **was not run** — this
container has no `node_modules`, and the session's only changes are one
markdown file. The next session should run it once as usual.

### Why

Sam opened with the rebuild "almost complete" and asked what was worth
working on, pointing first at the premium-group payment ids — "we use
the product name ending, which is really bad" — and suspecting the
remaining problems trace to the stack the product was first built on.
He was right, and that suspicion turned out to be the thread through
every finding: the legacy site did its thinking in the browser, and the
rebuild moved the *rendering* to the server while keeping the *data
shape*.

### What was decided

- **A new document, not `BUILD_LIST.md`** (Sam, explicitly, twice).
  `docs/product-plan/post-rebuild-diagnosis.md`. A line in BUILD_LIST
  means the work is real and ordered; a finding here means only that it
  has been seen. It carries its own rules, and a finding leaves for
  BUILD_LIST when approved or is marked ✖ with a reason when rejected,
  so nothing is re-found.
- **`rebuild.md` stays untouched.** Sam asked that the proposed §8 row
  live in the new document as text to be judged, not as an edit to the
  plan. It is there, marked draft.
- **Option C for D1** (the premium product ids): `programs.prep_product_id`,
  mirroring the existing `trial_product_id`. A and B recorded as
  rejected, with reasons.
- **S7 covers two tables, not one** (Sam, after spotting it himself):
  attempts *and* offline packs. One set of snapshot machinery.
- **Keep live messaging.** Sam's first instinct was to close it; the
  audit showed it is not the door (D10).
- **Do not adopt MyNclex's access gate.** Its bank is readable by any
  signed-in user; `user_has_course()` is the stricter of the two.

### Findings

D1 premium products found by the `_2026_PREP` id ending, where
`programs.trial_product_id` already does the same job with a column ·
D2 `products.kind` filtered on by nothing, three rival definitions of
"paid" · D3 `telegram_group_keys` free text, two mash values already in
the data, the Telegram gate would be built on it · D4 no Paystack
webhook — a payer who closes the tab has paid and got nothing · D5 the
runner hands every answer to the browser · D6 `answers_json` stores
`correct` a second time · D7 attempts' policies row-scoped but not
column-scoped, so a student can set their own score · D8 the bank
readable directly from the browser by a subscriber — the root · D9 both
builders ship a whole course's stems and rationales · D10 the browser's
database credential is inherent to the stack · D11 the admin bank reads
are a speed problem, not a leak · D12 a saved offline pack is a pointer
list, not the snapshot its own header calls it.

### What went wrong, and what it taught

- **I proposed touching `rebuild.md` §8 and Sam corrected it.** He had
  already said findings go in the new document; the §8 row belonged
  there too, as text. Simpler than what I proposed. The rule is now in
  the document's own rules block.
- **I appended D9–D12 after a `#` heading**, so four findings read as
  parts of the *Proposed direction* rather than as findings. Reordered:
  D1–D12, then the three cross-cutting sections, then the direction with
  the draft row last. Append order is not structure.
- **MyNclex is not uniformly better.** The temptation was to read it as
  the reference and copy it; on the access gate it is worse and openly
  defers the fix. The document carries a ⚠ not to harmonise ours away.
- **"C solves it" needed correcting before Sam committed to it.** C
  closes D5–D7 and D12 but not D8; it is what makes closing D8 possible.
  Said before the go-ahead, not after.

### Read for comparison

`Quademia/mynclex` was cloned read-only and read for the runner and
attempt architecture. AGENTS.md rule #2 bars *importing* from a sibling;
reading and copy-paste are allowed, and the document says what was read
and on what date. Its answer to D5 is a named principle — **Pillar 2, no
answer-key leakage** — enforced by a sealed/unsealed column projection at
the server boundary plus a type split that makes the mistake a compile
error, with a per-item unseal for instant feedback. Its attempts are a
header row plus one snapshot row per question; students hold SELECT only
and every write goes through a `SECURITY DEFINER` RPC; its builder returns
a count and a per-type breakdown, never content.

### What is open

- **Nothing is approved and nothing is queued.** The next decision is
  Sam's: tick S7 into `rebuild.md` §8, or leave it a draft. The timing
  argument is in the document — `rebuild.md` D5 leaves both attempts and
  offline packs **empty on launch day**, so both restructure windows
  close at cutover.
- **The order is fixed and matters:** S7 → D9 (builders to counts-only)
  → D8 (revoke the answer columns). The first two are not protection on
  their own; the third is, and cannot run before them.
- **Sam's next method, his words:** take each table, then what code uses
  it, then whether the table is good — and/or take each page and see
  what it leaks into the browser. Two sweeps; the second would extend
  the inventory in the document beyond the question bank.
- **The branch is unmerged on the remote.** Ask Sam before merging;
  delete the remote branch after its merge, per AGENTS.md.

## Session — 2026-09-16 (night) — **the legacy check: every legacy page compared with its rebuilt route; nothing missing outright; 14 behaviour gaps queued (4 to fix first), about 40 minor differences, 5 legacy bugs the rebuild fixed without a record; recorded in BUILD_LIST, nothing changed** — Claude

On `claude/work-session-76a753`, after the evening entry's merge, in the
same worktree; the dev server restarted for it and stopped at the end.
One docs commit. `npm run lint:check`: 0 known, 0 warnings, unchanged.
**No code changed.** Sam asked for the merge at the end; git records it.

### Why

Sam declared the rebuild's slices complete (the evening entry) and
said the next step was a surface-by-surface check of `legacy/` for
anything missed. He declined a checklist to work through himself, then
asked for the check to be done.

### How it was done

Nine read-only readers in parallel, each given its legacy pages, their
rebuilt routes, the documented differences to ignore (the UI
conventions, §9, §12, the After list) and one rule — cite both sides
(file:line) and search the rebuild before calling anything missing:
public and auth pages; student pages (two groups); offline packs; both
runners; admin pages (three groups); the shared shell, the 91 functions
of `mynmclicensure-api.js` and the database. The important findings
were then re-read in the code by me: 1–5, 7, 9–11, 13, 14 below, and the
Learning History retake. Nothing was run in a browser.

**Headline: nothing is missing outright.** Every legacy page, control,
message, live API function (71 with a caller, 4 dead), trigger, RPC,
bucket and config key has a counterpart.

### The gaps, as reported to Sam (all in `BUILD_LIST.md`, numbered)

**Fix first — students' answers, scores, or the decision to sign up:**

1. **Runner autosave restarts on every change.** The interval effect
   depends on `saveProgress`, a `useCallback` over the answers, flags
   and SATA checks, so every answer or flag clears the 60 s timer; a
   steadily answering student only saves on Previous / Next. A timed
   exam loses answers on a closed tab while the clock runs. Legacy's
   `setTimeout` loop saved every interval regardless. The timer tick
   beside it already uses `useEffectEvent` for exactly this reason.
2. **A dropped connection freezes the runner.** `submitQuiz`,
   `saveAndExit` and Send feedback await Server Actions with no
   try/catch: a rejected call leaves the overlay and spinner forever,
   no score card. Legacy's API returned an error and the page showed the
   local score. The audience has unreliable connections.
3. **Question Bank: MCQ → TF keeps the old answer letter.** `applyType`
   changes the type and shuffle only; the TF dropdown offers A and B, so
   a stored C–F displays as "A (True)" and saves as `d`. Legacy rebuilt
   the dropdown, resetting to A.
4. **Register's trial hint never shows** — `trialDays: null` with a
   comment still waiting on slice 8.

**Smaller, noticed:**

5. The student and admin layouts load the Messages badge, My Courses and
   the name / photo once; sidebar links are `next/link`, so a layout does
   not re-render — stale until a full load. The dropdowns' open state
   carries across pages too.
6. The device check on every Server Action (§10) signs a kicked or
   expired device out at its next save, losing unsaved answers; legacy
   checked only on page load. By design, the effect never written down;
   worse with 1.
7. Quiz Builder and Offline Pack builder: `statusOverride` is set and
   never cleared, so a failure message sticks; "Select a course…" skips
   the reload and leaves the old course's topics and counts.
8. Password reset: legacy's login page turned the lingering recovery
   session into a device session and landed the student on the
   dashboard; the rebuild signs out and asks for a sign-in. Sam to decide.
9. Admin Messages: the pane reads the active thread out of the loaded
   list, so a filter that hides it empties the pane while the realtime
   subscription keeps marking new student messages read; no way back on
   a phone.
10. Admin Payments: the panel is derived from the rows, so the reload
    after Retry Activation hides "Activated ✓" or closes the panel.
11. Admin Products: the form starts from the stored `courses_included`
    while the picker lists active courses only, so a draft or archived
    course is kept on save; legacy saved only the ticked boxes. Sam to
    rule.
12. Admin Users: Assign now refuses a deactivated account through the
    grant's check; legacy's direct insert did not.
13. `getConfigRows` and `getActiveSubscriptionsWithProduct` swallow a
    read error into an empty list: "No config keys found." and "No
    active subscriptions found yet." instead of legacy's errors.
14. The pack renderer looks the course up in `getCourses` (active only),
    so an inactive course prints its code; legacy used `getCourseById`.

### The minor differences (about 40; Sam's call whether to tidy)

- **Public and auth:** login's "← Back to Home" goes to the landing
  page; a signed-in visitor to /register is bounced to /router by the
  middleware (legacy showed the form); register's load failures lose
  "Could not load programmes / schools"; Premium Prep has no "No premium
  prep products are available yet." and no full-screen spinner while a
  payment starts.
- **Student:** Learning History's chip ✕ keeps the filters (a typed
  `?course=` address only); Profile's `?complete=1` re-opens and
  re-scrolls after each save; an announcement's body button marks the
  card read on screen at once; the message box doesn't shrink after
  sending; Fixed Quizzes / Mock Exams keep sections open through filter
  changes and Abandon, and show a raw database message on a database
  failure; the Quiz Builder's lost-course setup shows the course id.
- **Offline packs:** a failed Refresh keeps the loaded cards; "Use this"
  keeps the saved pack name; a lost-course setup opens "Course not
  available" with the id; the renderer's "Generated" date is formatted
  in UTC.
- **Runners:** Send feedback saves without "Progress saved" / "Save
  failed"; a "Loading your quiz…" overlay at Submit; fixed tab titles;
  no "Leave site?" on the preflight card; review mode's progress bar
  colour; the timed status line around start, resume and time-up.
- **Admin:** the dashboard sets no tab title; no "Saved ✓" pause on
  Products and Courses; Question Bank — the list blanks to "Loading
  questions…" after Save and jumps, replacing an image needs Remove
  first, an opened question isn't scrolled to, SATA letters are saved in
  click order (the pack key prints "E, A"); the announcement editor's
  student search doesn't close on an outside click and skips deactivated
  students, and "Title is required." doesn't focus the title; Admin
  Messages — the closed banner lost its Reopen button and wording, the
  empty pane's wording, the reply box height, New Thread's focus,
  "Unread only" drops an opened thread at once, the course picker's
  placeholder; no loading placeholders on admin pages.
- **Shell:** rows inside the dropdowns are bold with no accent
  background, and My Account opens on Profile and Upgrade; the student
  Messages badge counts open threads, where legacy's Messages page
  counted its own.
- Seen, the same in both: the Subscriptions page's Reference link to
  Payments carries `?ref=`, which neither Payments page reads.

### Legacy bugs the rebuild fixed without a record (keep; Sam to confirm)

Learning History's Retake always failed in legacy (its list query
omits `item_ids`, a NOT NULL column); Send feedback on a question with
"%" threw in legacy (the reference decoded twice); legacy's inbox never
loaded `read_by_user`, so any thread with a support reply looked
unread; the admin question picker's Subtopic filter reset itself before
being read; a timed exam saved before Start (autosave from page load,
or Save & Resume Later on the card) lost its pre-Start time. Smaller:
programme names instead of ids on Procedures and in the announcement
editor, the character counter's colour, the builders' no-access state.

**A note corrected:** the 6b entry says legacy "could never reach" the
pre-Start save path. It could, as above; the 6b fix is real.

### For cutover

The Supabase Auth dashboard settings on the shared project (redirect
allow-list, sender, the QAcademy-branded reset and magic-link
templates) are recorded nowhere in this repo, and the live address must
join the allow-list — a `BUILD_LIST.md` line beside Cutover; the
dashboard was not looked at. `db/schema.sql` and `db/rls.sql` lag the
migrations (offline packs, announcements); the database is complete.

### What is open

- **The fourteen gaps**, 1–4 first, one at a time; 8 and 11 need Sam's
  word first; the minor list and the five kept fixes wait on Sam.
- Everything the evening entry left open.

## Session — 2026-09-16 (evening) — **10 built: email — the four legacy emails (welcome, access active, access removed, complete your setup) sent from the server through Resend, with the sender as MyNclex's, the Users page's Assign emailing too, and every value escaped (§9 #25); walked by Sam, all six arrived; then the rebuild's slices declared complete — 15 dropped as a slice, 16 Cutover and 17 Telegram gate moved to After the rebuild** — Claude

records it, not this entry.**

### The reading (Sam: "let's talk about slice 10, no coding yet")

Legacy's email Worker read whole (`index.js`, the four templates, the

subscriptions.html` `submitGrant` and `confirmCancel`, `admin/
