`docs/product-plan/transactional-email.md` — the sending identity agreed
2026-08-12 and not yet done (`hello@mail.quademia.com`, one subdomain
for every product), Resend's free plan at 100 a day with Pro "before
real signup volume", and its own note that gamma's templates paste
`{{name}}` in raw.

Already in place: `appOrigin()` (9a). Two screens already promised an
email nobody sent — the register success screen and the Grant dialog's
note. `RESEND_API_KEY` was empty in every `.env.local`.

### The rulings (Sam, 2026-09-16) — asked one at a time, at Sam's request

1. **The sender is MyNclex's as it is today**: `MyNMCLicensure
   <noreply@quademia.com>`, replies to `support@quademia.com` through a
   Reply-To header. Rejected: no reply address (legacy's), and
   "Quademia" as the display name. Sam first proposed reusing MyNclex's
   Resend keys (same account); the case put for the product's own two
   — a MyNclex key replaced or revoked would silently stop these emails,
   and this app has no email page to show it; Resend tells keys apart;
   a key is shown once, so reuse means finding where it was saved. Sam
   made `licensure-dev-app` and `licensure-prod-app`, pasted the dev one
   into both `.env.local` files and set both Worker secrets himself.
2. **The Users page's Assign sends SUBSCRIPTION_ASSIGNED too** — the
   email lives in the grant, which both pages call since 14a (§9 #24's
   one mechanism). Rejected: a switch so only Grant sends it, as legacy.
3. **§9 #25, fixed in this slice**: every value filled into a template is
   escaped. A stranger could otherwise register someone else's address
   with a link in the First name box and the owner would get a genuine
   welcome email carrying it. Rejected: carrying the raw paste.
4. **The footer**: "Quademia", with no company name (Quademia Ltd is not
   registered — MyNclex's rule), the site link to quademia.com (per
   environment through `parentSiteOrigin()`); **the Telegram button goes
   to `t.me/QAcademynurseshub`**, the live channel (811 subscribers) the
   landing page and the sidebar use — legacy's footer pointed at
   `t.me/qacademynurses`, a blank "Contact" page with no name or
   picture (both fetched to check). Rejected: keeping "QAcademy
   Educational Consult" (UI convention #5).

Already the plan's (§7.2), no ruling needed: the expiry read back from
the row; QAcademy → Quademia; the 9a support address.

All five written into §12's slice 10 paragraph and §9 #25 in the first
commit, before any code.

### Built

`lib/email/send.ts` — one function: a plain fetch to Resend (no SDK,
MyNclex's reason), awaited by the caller, never throwing; a missing key,
a refusal or a network failure is one log line and the action carries
on. A 10-second timeout so a hung call cannot hold a registration or an
admin's button. The log names the subject and Resend's id, never the
address. No outbox, retry or email page — legacy had none (MyNclex's
are a mechanism, not stack).

`lib/email/templates/` — `shared.ts` (the support address, `esc()`, the
`{ subject, html }` shape), `footer.ts`, and the four templates, each
legacy's HTML transcribed with its Worker subject.

The call sites: `app/register/actions.ts` step 5, before the sign-out as
legacy (the programme's `program_name`, readable by anyone); the grant
in `lib/subscriptions/actions.ts` (the student's email and names and the
product's kind now selected, the new id held so the row can be read
back) and the revoke (the student and the product read in parallel),
each email inside its own try; **`retryActivationAction` in
`lib/payments/admin-actions.ts`** — admin-gated, runs the same
`verifyPayment` (its rate limit and its answers unchanged), and on
SETUP_REQUIRED with an email sends the setup link on `appOrigin()` with
the token and `SETUP_TOKEN_LIFETIME_MS` as the 48 hours. It exists
because the confirmation page polls verify every 3 s: an email inside
verify would go out on every poll. The Payments page calls it; comments
only in `subscriptions-client.tsx` and `wrangler.jsonc`.

### Carried as legacy had it

- **The access-active email names the product as legacy's Grant dropdown
  labelled it, `name (KIND)`** — "Registered Nursing Free Full Access
  (FREE)". Flagged to Sam before the walk; he did not ask for a change,
  so it is carried and **not** on the After list.
- The welcome says the trial is active whether or not one was granted;
  "Hi ," with an empty name; the footer's fixed "© 2026".
- No email when a student pays through Paystack; the setup email only
  from the admin's Retry; a failed send is lost with a log line.
- The setup link carries the token the page never reads (§9 #20).

### What went wrong and what it taught

- **The pane cannot screenshot or read a local `file://` page** ("a
  static snapshot"; page tools refuse it). The rendered email was seen
  by writing its HTML into a localhost tab with `document.write`.
- **A scratch render outside Next**: `esbuild` (a dev dependency)
  bundled a scratch entry with `--tsconfig=tsconfig.json`, so `@/`
  resolved, and `node` wrote the four emails to files — subjects, links,
  the date and the escaping checked before any send.
- **A `cd legacy/…` inside a Bash call moved the session's working
  directory**, twice; absolute paths after.
- **My shell's `wrangler` has no Cloudflare login** (non-interactive, no
  `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN`), so Sam's two Worker secrets could not be
  confirmed from here. The dev Worker's first email after the merge is
  the proof.
- Seen, not this slice's: `AuthApiError: Invalid Refresh Token` four
  times in the dev log around `/login` — a stale sign-in cookie being
  refreshed by the middleware; the visitor is treated as signed out.
  Not investigated.
- Seen, not a defect: the walk's trial row ran 60 days and one hour —
  `trial.ts` adds days on the machine's calendar, and this laptop leaves
  BST on 25 October. The Worker runs UTC and Ghana keeps GMT.

### The walk (Sam, his own browser on `localhost:3000`, Gmail + tags)

1. Registered `+slice10` → WELCOME_STUDENT.
2. **Grant** RN Free Full Access (a new row, expires 16 Oct 2026) →
   SUBSCRIPTION_ASSIGNED.
3. **Grant** again, the same product (extended, **15 Nov 2026**, read
   back from the row — legacy's page would have said 16 Oct) → the
   same email.
4. **Assign** on the Users page, the RM trial (15 Nov 2026) → the same
   email — ruling 2 proven.
5. **Revoke** the RM trial → SUBSCRIPTION_REVOKED.
6. A PAID row with no account made by SQL (`QAC_10E3A11D5E7F`,
   `+slice10pay`, RN Full Access, GHS 150) → **Retry Activation** →
   PAYMENT_SETUP_REQUIRED; its button opened `/payment-confirmation`
   with the reference and the token, and the page's own verify sent
   nothing — one setup email in the log.

Six sends, each logged with Resend's id; **Sam: all arrived**, with the
dates above and working buttons. Before the walk the welcome was viewed

On `claude/work-session-150e66` from `main` at `d0a8a47`, in the
desktop-app worktree `work-session-90f627`. One feature commit and this
docs commit. `npm run lint:check`: 0 known, 0 warnings, unchanged;

`BUILD_LIST.md` and §14 — on the walk below. **The merge to `main` was
asked for at the end and is recorded by git, not here.**

### The question that opened the session (Sam, 2026-09-16)

Sam asked whether **slice 15, the phone pass, should stay in the
rebuild at all**, given how much sits on the "After the rebuild" list
and will change things again later.

The answer put to him: 15 is not where the phone work happens. UI
convention #3 already makes every slice carry its own phone layout, and
every slice since 7c has been walked at 375px as it landed — 28 of the
32 stylesheets carry a phone breakpoint, and the four that do not
(admin dashboard, student announcements, procedures, the dashboard's
own, now fixed) are simple stacked pages rather than proven defects. So
15 is a **sweep**, not a build: walk the whole student flow on a real
phone, catch what each page missed alone, fix the CSS. Against Sam's
worry: the After-the-rebuild list is almost entirely **behaviour**
(a missing link, a filter's scope, a stats bar's totals, token quirks),
not layout, so a pass done at the end survives it. And the audience is
Ghanaian nursing students on phones — cutover (16) is the moment the
product meets them, so the walk has to happen before it whether or not
it carries a number. **Sam's call: keep 15, keep it where it is** (last
but one, after 7f and 10). It stays a named slice rather than a bullet
inside cutover, because a named slice with "Sam walks the student flow
on a phone" as its done-when is harder to skip under pressure.

Sam then picked 7f.

### The reading

Legacy `student/dashboard.html` read whole, 919 lines including the
script block, plus the pieces already built that it depends on: 11b's
`AnnouncementsStrip` (checked against legacy's `loadAnnouncements`
rules — always visible, unread only, two at most, pinned auto-expanded,
the two empty states, the ✕ that marks read; a faithful match, reused
untouched), `getStudentCourseAccess`, `getCourses`, 7a's `runnerHref`,
7d's days-left box, and the student sidebar's `dynamic: 'courses'` row
— which is why legacy's `populateCourseDropdown` has no counterpart
here: the shell fills that list for every student page, not the
dashboard.

Ten blocks; two already existed (the header from 2b, the announcements
block from 11b), eight were the slice.

### The ruling asked for and given (Sam, 2026-09-16)

Legacy's dashboard sent **every** attempt's Review and Resume to
`instant.html`, timed ones included — so a timed mock resumed in the
wrong runner. It is a real defect, it is **not** in §9, and AGENTS.md
says to log and ask rather than fix silently. It was put to Sam with

### Built

`app/(app)/student/dashboard/page.tsx` — the placeholder card replaced
by legacy's page in its order. A Server Component but for the nudge and
11b's block. The subscription bar reads the access map: no access → the
red bar with Subscribe Now into 9b's upgrade page; otherwise "Platform
Access / Active" with the longest expiry, amber at seven days or fewer.
The course cards, the two promo blocks with legacy's copy, the
seven-column attempts table with its status chips, and the floating
Portal Guide bubble.

`app/(app)/student/dashboard/profile-nudge.tsx` — the ✕ and legacy's
`qa_profile_nudge_hidden` session key. The **server** decides whether
the strip is offered: the gate already carries `phone_number`,
`school_id` and `school_other`, so legacy's second read of `users` is
not made (14a's precedent — the join instead of the second lookup).

`lib/attempts`: `getRecentAttempts` and `RECENT_ATTEMPTS_LIMIT` —
legacy's `.limit(5)` with the list row's columns instead of `*`.

`styles/student-dashboard.css` — the rest of legacy's inline `<style>`
scoped under `.sdash`, legacy's global `.btn` / `.btn-primary` scoped in
as the other student pages do, legacy's own 560px rule for the channel
buttons, and a 768px pass.

### Carried as legacy had it

- **"Expires 14 Oct" when the subscription row says 13 Oct.** The access
  map rounds the remaining time up to whole days (28) and rebuilds the
  date as today plus 28. Legacy's dashboard read the same map, and 7d's
  "28 days left" is the same number. Flagged to Sam during the walk; he
  did not ask for a change, so it is carried and **not** on the After
  list. Raise it again if the true date is wanted.
- The announcements block's first empty state has no "View all" footer
  and the second does.
- The two promo buttons are full width — legacy's inline styles set no
  width, so the global `.btn-primary { width: 100% }` reaches them.

### Changed on purpose

- The runner link follows the attempt's mode (the ruling above).
- The course card is an anchor, not a div with an `onclick`.
- Dates are formatted on the **server**. Ghana keeps GMT all year and
  the Worker runs UTC, so a reader sees the same day legacy's
  browser-side formatting gave them; 7d already does this. In dev the
  machine's own zone applies, which is why a 00:23 UTC attempt showed
  01:23 in the walk.

### What went wrong and what it taught

- **`react-hooks/set-state-in-effect`.** The nudge's first draft read
  `sessionStorage` in a `useEffect` and called `setShown` — refused by
  the React compiler's lint. Reading storage during render instead
  would split the server's paint from the browser's (the 9a hydration
  lesson). The answer is `useSyncExternalStore` with a module-level
  subscribe, a `getSnapshot` that reads storage in a try/catch, and a
  **server snapshot of "hidden"** — which is also what legacy's own
  markup did (`display:none` until its script ran). **AGENTS.md
  corrected**: the old bullet said to stamp the clock into state "from a
  handler or an effect", and an effect body is exactly what this lint
  refuses. Two bullets now: the three lint rules with handler-only
  advice, and browser storage through `useSyncExternalStore`.
- **Legacy's global `.btn-primary` sets `width: 100%`, and the page
  overrode it inline, per button.** Scoping those rules into a
  stylesheet loses the inline's precedence, and two buttons rendered
  wrong in opposite directions: Subscribe Now (legacy inline
  `width:auto`) stretched across the bar until its rule was raised to
  `.sdash .btn.sub-cta`; the two promo buttons had been given a
  `width:auto` legacy never had, which happened to render correctly only
  because it lost on source order — removed rather than left as a rule
  that says one thing and does another. **When transcribing a legacy
  page, an inline style is a higher-specificity rule, not a suggestion.**
- A 0.75-scale screenshot caught the page before hydration and the nudge
  looked missing; it was in the DOM. Legacy behaved the same way. Check
  the DOM before believing a screenshot about a post-hydration element.

### The walk (in the pane, on Sam's two sign-ins)

**As admin** (no phone, no school, no subscription, no attempts — the
empty half of every block): the nudge, its link to
`/student/profile?complete=1`, the ✕ writing the session key, the strip
still gone after a reload; the red bar with Subscribe Now; "🔔 Nothing
from us yet"; "No courses available yet."; the empty attempts state with
its Custom Quiz and Fixed Quizzes buttons; the guide bubble. All nine
links audited against legacy's destinations. 375px: no horizontal
scroll (`scrollWidth === clientWidth === 375`), everything stacked.

**As student1**: no nudge (the profile is complete); "Platform Access /
Active — Expires 14 Oct 2026"; "🎉 You're all caught up!" with its
footer; the three course cards — GP with its five programme badges,
RN_MED, RN_SURG — each opening the course page, General Paper showing
"28 days left"; the five newest attempts matching the rows read by SQL,
with their scores, dates and chips. **The ruling proven twice**: every
timed attempt's Review points at `/runner/timed`, and clicking the
newest one opened the **timed** runner in review mode ("📖 Review Mode —
Answers are read-only"); the in-progress instant attempt reads Resume
and points at `/runner/instant`. No console errors on either sign-in.

### The performance investigation (Sam, 2026-09-16)

After 7f was walked and ticked, Sam asked why the app is slow, and said
he thought it slower than MyNclex "which is even a bigger app". Then,
separately: "I think it may also be the database structure that plays a
factor — I may be wrong." Two multi-agent investigations were run, each
fanning out probes and then putting every candidate cause through two
adversarial passes. Nothing was changed: read-only throughout.

**The answer: the app waits, it does not work.** A warm
`/student/dashboard` costs 1.3–2.0 s; `/login`, which is
unauthenticated, costs 149 ms. The gap is round trips, not computation.
About **thirteen** calls to Supabase per page load, **six of them
strictly sequential**. Postgres itself accounts for roughly **13 ms** of
a 1,500 ms page — the database is not the bottleneck at this data size.

The measured causes, largest first:

- **The gate waits three times in a row** (`lib/access/internal.ts:31`):
  who are you → your profile row → your device-session row, each waiting
  on the last. **450–650 ms.** The third is slice 2a's two-device limit —
  a real feature, and the reason the chain is three deep rather than two.
- **The middleware asks the auth server again on every request**
  (`middleware.ts:58`), prefetches included — **170–290 ms**, and it
  fires about 4.7× more often than it needs to.
- **Thirteen calls in six waves**, some queries running two or three
  times over because the layout and the page share nothing — **300–450 ms.**
- **The unread badge is two serial queries** and finishes last, so it
  sets the finish line (`lib/messaging/queries.ts:62`). One verifier
  confirmed it, one called the magnitude overstated; recorded as
  35–200 ms with that split noted.
- **The browser side**: the avatar is served at full size — 1.38 MB for
  a 24-pixel circle, re-fetched every page — there is no loading state
  anywhere, and ~34 in-page links are plain anchors, so a click throws
  the page away and re-runs all thirteen calls.

**Cleared, having been suspected:** page weight (9–14 KB of JS per
route, 5.5 KB CSS; the server's 2–4 KB reply takes ~2,000 ms to arrive —
one byte per millisecond) and the database. Six of the nine
`auth_rls_initplan` advisor warnings are false alarms: Postgres already
hoists those.

**The MyNclex comparison is real.** The repo was found at
`C:\Users\confi\qacademy-mynclex` and read. Same stack, same versions,
bigger codebase. Three differences, all provable: its gate waits **twice,
in parallel**, where this one waits three times in sequence; it has **no
layout wrapping its student pages**, where this app's student layout runs
three queries before the page beneath may start; and it uses `next/link`
in **48 files** against this app's **3**, which is why clicks here feel
like reloads — they are. ⚠ **Not measured:** nobody ran MyNclex's dev
server and timed it, so there is no MyNclex page time to set beside the
1.5 s. Do not let a number be invented later.

**How much is dev-only.** The `next.js:` figures in the dev log — 12.3 s
on `/student/course/GP`, 3.5 s on `/admin/messages` — are Turbopack
compiling a route on first visit and do not exist in production; warm
loads are already ~20 ms. Dev-mode React is 100–250 ms. Roughly half the
per-trip network cost is the laptop-to-Ireland hop. **What survives:** the
number of trips and their order — every authenticated page is
`force-dynamic` (38 files), so production repeats all thirteen with no
cache to rescue it. Estimated production dashboard today: **0.6–0.9 s**,
unchanged, without anyone touching a line. `npm run cf:dev` would give a
real number instead of an estimate; not run this session.

**Worth knowing:** signing in takes about **6.6 s** button-to-dashboard,
because the login action, `/router` and the dashboard each pay the auth
chain in turn.

**Governance.** None of the fixes changes what the app does or shows —
all internal plumbing. But `AGENTS.md` ⭐ allows an internal shape change
only where `rebuild.md` §8 lists it *and* Sam has ticked it, and **§8 does
not list the auth path at all**. So the two auth-path fixes are not
merely unapproved, they have nowhere to be approved yet: they need a new
§8 entry first. A `BUILD_LIST.md` line under the open §8 decisions says
so. The five safe items are ⬜ "Speed:" lines under *After the rebuild*,
whose heading was widened to admit internal findings, since "product
changes spotted while transcribing legacy" no longer described all of it.


**The schema, asked separately.** Sam's second question got its own
investigation. Verdict: **partly right, but not about the dashboard.**
Postgres does 5–25 ms on that page, under 2% of the wait, and the
eleven item tables, the TEXT blobs and the `U_` ids cost it nothing —
the dashboard opens no question table and reads no answer blob.

- **The one real schema cost today is a rule, not a shape.** The
  item-bank policy's `user_has_course()` is written so Postgres re-runs
  it **once per question row** — ~0.22 ms × ~900 rows = **210–230 ms**
  per Quiz Builder course pick, ~130 ms on the admin bank, ~20 ms on a
  100-question runner load, **0 ms on the dashboard**. Linear: at a
  10,000-row course it is ~2.2 s per read. The fix is eleven
  `ALTER POLICY` lines — no table, column, type or row changes, and
  byte-identical results. ⚠ The 10–20× gain is inferred from Postgres's
  documented behaviour, **not yet measured** on a rewritten policy;
  measure straight after the migration rather than quoting a number.
- **This rule is ours, not legacy's.** Legacy had no entitlement check
  on questions at all (§9 defect 3). The rebuild added it correctly and
  wrote one line Postgres cannot hoist. Fixing it undoes nothing.
- **Supabase's advisor is blind here.** It flags nine policies as slow —
  the nine *cheapest* in the schema, worth microseconds — and misses all
  eleven expensive ones, because it only recognises Supabase's own
  function names and this app wrote its own. Working down that list in
  order would fix nothing. Do not treat the advisor as the agenda.
- **§8 S2 (eleven item tables): keeping is right, and it is marginally
  *faster* than one table here** — the app reads one course at a time,
  so small tables mean never walking past other courses' rows. Merging
  would make the policy problem worse. The split's real price is
  maintenance: eleven policies to fix, and no way to count the bank
  without eleven questions.
- **§8 S3 (blobs → JSONB): recommended to leave unticked as a
  performance decision.** It buys zero milliseconds, would slightly slow
  the runner's autosave (a 100-question mock rewrites the blob ~200
  times) and grows storage ~12%. Tick it one day for the *feature* it
  unlocks — "which questions are most often wrong" cannot be asked of a
  text blob — not for speed. That is an after-cutover product call.

**Two correctness cliffs found on the way, neither of them slowness:**
several admin queries collect every matching student id into one
`.in(...)` list, which exceeds what the API accepts at roughly
**2,000–5,000 students** — Bulk Send and the admin inbox then return
nothing rather than erroring; and the admin Attempts page computes its
analytics from at most 5,000 loaded rows, so past that it silently
reports numbers from an arbitrary slice. **Wrong, not late** — that
second one is the finding I would least want discovered after launch.
Both are app code, both have `BUILD_LIST.md` lines.

**Left unverified, deliberately:** the shared-project theory (gamma
hosts this schema plus the live legacy product plus MyTeacher) came back
**false on dev** — that instance is 99.95% idle — but could not be tested
on **production**, where the tooling refused reads. Worth one query
before launch. And per-request travel to Supabase swung wildly during
measurement: the same 11-row query took anywhere from 17 ms to 1,365 ms,
which dwarfs every schema effect measured and is a better lead than any
table shape.

**A discrepancy, left standing:** the two investigations counted the
dashboard's round trips as thirteen and as sixteen. Neither was
reconciled; the shape of the answer does not turn on it.

### What is open

- **Slice 7 is closed** — 7a through 7f all built.
- Remaining: **10** (email — nothing is wired at all yet, and it is the
  last link in the 8 → 9 → 10 chain), **15** (the phone pass, kept where
  it is), **16** (cutover), **17** (Telegram gate).
- The prod pause decision still stands open in `BUILD_LIST.md`: the 21
  paid products are archived on prod, so nothing sells until those rows
  are set active.
- The "Expires 14 Oct vs 13 Oct" rounding above, if Sam wants it changed.
- **From the investigations, in the order I would take them.** Nothing
  was changed; every item is a `BUILD_LIST.md` line, none is approved.
  1. The eleven item-bank policies — the biggest database win available
     and the cheapest change found anywhere. Measure the gain rather
     than quoting the inferred 10–20×.
  2. The two correctness cliffs (the `.in()` id lists, the admin
     Attempts 5,000-row cap). Not slowness; they arrive with the first
     few thousand students and the second reports wrong numbers quietly.
  3. The three safe speed items: a route-level loading state, the
     1.38 MB avatar, the `next/link` sweep.
  4. The auth-path pair — **blocked until §8 gains an entry and Sam
     ticks it.** They are the largest single saving (620–940 ms) and the
     most sensitive code in the product; they want their own slice.
- `npm run cf:dev` was **not** run. Until it is, every production figure
  in this entry is an estimate.
- §8 S3 now has an evidenced recommendation (leave unticked for speed,
  tick only for the analytics feature). Still Sam's to decide; the
  `BUILD_LIST.md` line is untouched.

On `claude/slice-9a-payments` from `main` at the session-end docs

is recorded by git, not here.**

### The reading and the split (Sam, 2026-09-15)

Sam picked slice 9 ("lets look into 9"). Read whole: the payments
Worker (2,052 lines — the four public routes, the four admin ones
already rebuilt in slice 8, the helpers), doc 01, §7.1, legacy
`subscribe.html`, `premium-prep.html`'s payment call,
`payment-confirmation.html`, `student/upgrade.html`, `admin/payments.html`
and the two API helpers behind it, the legacy schema and RLS for
`payments`. The picture put to Sam: four parts (starting a payment
through two doors, confirming it with a polling page, the setup form
that creates the account, the admin page with the rescue buttons).
Too big for one session → **9a** the money path, **9b** the two
management surfaces (the student upgrade page live through
init-upgrade, the admin Payments page); the upgrade page therefore
moved out of 7f into 9b, and 7f is the dashboard alone. Last session's
7f / 7g idea is not needed. Recorded in §12, §14 and BUILD_LIST in the
first commit.

Three things asked and answered before the build: the Paystack test
key (Sam pasted it — it landed on the Resend line of the main
checkout's `.env.local` and not in the worktree's copy; I could not
move it myself, the env files are off-limits to my tools, so Sam
fixed both by hand); the support address on the confirmation page
(`support@quademia.com`, the message opens "Hi Quademia Support"; the
WhatsApp button stays hidden as legacy's number was blank); two
legacy quirks found in the reading, carried and recorded as **§9 #20**
(the copied setup link carries a token the page never reads; verify
re-mints one on every call, so the link works anyway) and **§9 #21**
(verify is unauthenticated by design and hands a setup token to any
reference holder; the reference is the only secret), both under
"After the rebuild". Sam also asked whether `lib/payments` duplicates
`lib/money`: no — one is the formatter every price goes through, the
other is the Paystack flow; §7.1 and the folder rules name both.

### 9a — built

`db/migrations/20260915120000_payments.sql` (applied on dev by the
runner): `payments` with legacy's nineteen columns and S4 keys to
`products`, `users` (nullable — a pay-first row has no user until
setup) and `subscriptions` (nullable until activation); ADMIN-only
SELECT as legacy; **no INSERT or UPDATE policy on purpose** — every
write came from the Worker with the service role and now comes from
the Server Actions with the same key. `rate_limits` (one row per key)
and `check_payment_rate_limit(p_key, p_limit = 5, p_window_seconds = 60)`
SECURITY DEFINER, a fixed window, EXECUTE revoked from anon and
authenticated; the caller fails open on error, as the Worker did.
`schema.sql` and `rls.sql` regenerated.

`lib/payments/`: `types.ts` (the row, the four replies as
discriminated unions mirroring the Worker's JSON — what was an HTTP
status plus an error code is `ok` plus `error`; the constants);
`ids.ts` (`QAC_` + 12, `U_` + 10 for an account made at setup — the
Worker's length, kept beside register's 16 as the two SUB_ makers
are, the 32-hex token); `paystack.ts` (initialize and verify, the key
read inside each call); `rate-limit.ts` (the address from the same
headers `request-info` reads, `'unknown'` locally as the Worker's
was); `queries.ts` (the Worker's lookups over the service-role client);
`activate.ts` (the three modes, in order: `existing_by_ref` keeps the
original `activated_utc`; `extended` adds `duration_days` to the
ACTIVE row, resets `expiry_reminded`, stamps the new reference;
`created`; the product lookup here does not require active);
`init-public.ts`, `init-upgrade.ts` (requireStudent() in place of the
Bearer dance; wired by 9b), `verify.ts`, `setup-complete.ts` — each
the Worker's checks in its order with its messages. `lib/site/app-origin.ts`:
`appOrigin()` from `APP_ORIGIN` with the prod value as fallback — the
Paystack callback is `${appOrigin()}/payment-confirmation`; slice 10's
emails will use the same function.

`app/subscribe/`: the pay-first page — the programmes and products by
the anon client, whether a session is present (the "use the Upgrade
page" box); the client: the paid products (not a programme's trial,
price above zero — 21 of the 32 on dev), the pickers, the price box
through `formatMinor`, the remembered localStorage values under the
legacy keys, Proceed to Payment → the action → Paystack's checkout.
`app/payment-confirmation/`: public, nothing read on the server; the
client: the reference from `reference` / Paystack's `trxref` / `ref`
or storage, verify on arrival and every 3 s up to 20 times while
Paystack has not said success, the four screens with the legacy words,
Retry Verification, the setup form (first name, surname, password
twice, phone, the page's own five programme options), Hide Setup Form,
the support box, the amount line. `app/premium-prep/premium-prep-client.tsx`:
the inert line replaced by the action call and the redirect. Two
scoped stylesheets (`.subp`, `.pcf`).

**Changed on purpose:** the two pages' inline banners and the setup
form's message boxes are the shared toast (UI convention #1) — the
confirmation page's own status boxes stay, they are the screen, not a
message; prices through `formatMinor` (#4); "QAcademy" → "Quademia"
(#5); the paused panels are gone (the switch is lifted); the Worker's
`err.message` no longer reaches the browser on a Paystack or database
failure — the row's `failure_note` keeps it, the page gets the generic
line (§7.1); the Worker ignored the browser's callback on init-public
and built its own — here `appOrigin()`.

**Carried as legacy had it:** the subscribe page's odd copy "This
matches the rebuilt payment flow in your new stack." (a dev note that
reached the reader in legacy); the code-as-message replies
(`product_not_found_or_inactive` shown as the text, unreachable from
the pages); after setup the payer is not signed in — "Go to Login";
`setup-complete` captures no school, no referral, grants no trial
(§7.1 ⚠); the extension overwrites the subscription's `source_ref`
with the newest reference, so the replay guard is keyed on the last
payment only (harmless while a row never leaves ACTIVATED).

**One thing legacy did not do (Sam, 2026-09-15: after the rebuild):**
if the profile row fails after the login is created at setup, the
login is deleted again — the §9 #4 rollback the registration action
already has — because an orphan login would make every retry fail
with "already registered". It stays in the code; a line under "After
the rebuild" says it is Sam's to confirm or drop.

### What went wrong and what it taught

- **The in-app Browser pane cannot complete a Paystack checkout**:
  Paystack's page loads but its API calls are blocked by the pane
  ("ERR_BLOCKED_BY_CLIENT" in the console). The redirect itself, the
  INIT row and the access code were proven; the card step is Sam's,
  in his own browser. For the server side without a card, a row was
  set PAID by SQL (the `raw.verify` note says so) — the same shape
  Paystack's success leaves.
- **A hydration mismatch** on the confirmation page: the support
  link's message read localStorage during render behind a
  `typeof window` check, so the server and the first paint differed.
  The email is state set after mount now. Found by the dev overlay's
  "1 issue" badge.
- **The dev log printed a payer's password.** Next's development
  server prints a Server Action's first arguments, and
  `completePaymentSetup` took a plain object — Sam's test password
  appeared in the terminal ("that's dangerous"). Fix commit: the
  action takes a FormData, as register and login do; a FormData prints
  as `{}`. Proven on Sam's next setup. Rule of thumb for every future
  action that carries a secret: FormData in, never a plain object.
- **The 3-second poll and the 5-per-minute limit fight each other**
  (legacy's numbers, both transcribed): on an unpaid reference the
  page polled four times after the first check, then the sixth call
  was refused and the screen turned to "Too many requests" with Retry.
  Legacy behaved the same on Cloudflare; it reaches a real payer whose
  mobile-money payment is still pending after about twelve seconds.
  Sam: carry, list under "After the rebuild".
- Locally the rate limit's key is `payments:unknown` for every
  caller (no proxy header), so a dev walk shares one bucket; the row
  was deleted by SQL between steps.

### The first production release (Sam, 2026-09-15)

After the 9 merge Sam chose a first release over slice 10 ("dev is now
far ahead while nothing has been done about prod", and slice 10's
email key will need a prod Worker to live on). My view, given with the
facts and taken: a rehearsal, not a launch — prod answers only on its
workers.dev address (no DNS until §11), the migrations touch only
`licensure_gh` on the shared prod project, and a dry-run merge of
`main` into `production` had no conflicts (`production`'s twelve
own commits are the gamma-era release merges). The rule followed: a
pull request, a merge commit, on Sam's word — PR #1, merged at
`b325a96`. Both prod workflows green on the first run ever:
`migrate-prod` applied the eleven files in order ("0 applied, 11
pending" → all done, ~6 s); `deploy-prod` created `licensure-prod`,
which answers 200. The content copies landed on prod: 5 programmes,
141 schools, 31 products (counted through the API as anon). Sam then
set the two Worker secrets by hand (`SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` — the
prod project's; `PAYSTACK_SECRET_KEY` — the test key, the live one
parked). **Seen on prod:** the subscribe page lists no paid product,
and that is the live data, not a defect — all 21 paid products are
`archived` on prod (legacy paused paid plans by archiving them; the
five free and five trial products are active); the BUILD_LIST pause
line now says so. No admin exists on prod (D5: no data migration; an
admin is a promoted row, a cutover task), so the admin pages were not
part of the release test. The smoke test is Sam's (a registration):
recorded below when done.

### The branches on the remote (Sam's question, 2026-09-15)

Sam asked why the remote held several branches: twelve merged session
branches, pushed by earlier sessions and never removed. Cleaned up at
his word (the twelve deleted from the remote; the merged local ones
deleted too, except the three other worktrees still hold). My first
rule for it — delete the session branch after its merge — was wrong,
as Sam said: a session may merge to `main` several times from the
same branch. Read how MyNclex does it (its rules file, fetched from
GitHub, and its memory notes): the session branch is never pushed;
work reaches the remote by merging into `main` and pushing `main`;
origin carries `main` and `prod` only; a branch pushed by mistake is
deleted after its merge; and a session start lists the remote
branches, because a cloud session can push one. AGENTS.md rewritten
that way (the wrong commit dropped first) and merged.

## Session — 2026-09-14 (late night, seventh) — **fix: a photo over 1 MB failed on the new profile page — Next's Server Action body cap; raised to 3 MB, the refusal caught into legacy's "Upload failed" toast, the rule added to AGENTS.md; the same cap covered the question bank's image upload** — Claude

On `claude/fix-upload-body-limit` from `main` at the 7e docs commit
(`26dfcf8`), the same worktree and session, on Sam's report right
after the 7e merge: "I am uploading a profile picture and it's
failing; the log says exceeded 1 MB." One fix commit and this docs
commit. `npm run lint:check`: 0 known, 0 warnings, unchanged;
`tsc --noEmit` clean. **The merge to `main` was asked for at the end
and is recorded by git, not here.**

### What it was

Next caps the body of a Server Action at 1 MB by default. A photo
uploaded through `savePersonalDetails` travels inside that body, so
anything over about 1 MB was refused before the action ran — the dev
log: "Error: Body exceeded 1 MB limit … statusCode: 413". Legacy
allowed 2 MB and never met the cap because the browser sent the file
straight to storage. The 7e walk missed it because the test image was
under 2 KB. Two knock-ons: the page showed nothing (the refusal is a
thrown error, not a reply, so no toast; the button just went back to
"Save Changes"), and the admin Question Bank's rationale-image upload
(4a) goes through a Server Action the same way with the same 2 MB
limit — the same latent defect, reaching an admin after cutover.

### What changed

`next.config.ts`: `experimental.serverActions.bodySizeLimit: '3mb'` —
the product's 2 MB uploads plus the other fields and the multipart
framing. `profile-client.tsx`: the action call is wrapped so a thrown
refusal shows legacy's "Upload failed: Image must be JPG, PNG, or WebP
under 2 MB." toast. `AGENTS.md`: the rule under Known Workarounds
(every future upload through a Server Action meets this cap); the
file's date bumped. The dev server was restarted for the config.

### Proven in the pane (Sam's student1 sign-in — 2026-09-14)

A 700 × 700 noise PNG of 1.61 MB (canvas-made, through a DataTransfer)
on the profile page's file input → Save → the action ran (the dev
log) and the bucket object `student_U_73A9F6316E011A9E.png` is now
1,686,702 bytes (by SQL). The page kept showing the earlier picture:
the browser cached the same address — the "After the rebuild" quirk
listed in the 7e session, now seen. **Not walked:** the toast on a
refusal above 3 MB (the page's own 2 MB check stops it first), the
question bank's upload at the new cap.

### The cached photo — investigated and fixed (Sam, 2026-09-14)

Sam then reported "some photos are not showing, just an S letter".
Checked: the bucket held Sam's first upload as `…jpg` (182 KB, an
orphan — the file is named by extension, as legacy named it) and his
second as `…png` (1.58 MB, 1024 × 1024, the row's address); the "S"
was the 7e walk's 96 px test image, served from cache — the storage
delivery network answers a public object with `max-age=3600` and the
browser keeps it too, so the same address kept showing the previous
photo (a forced fetch of the same address in the pane returned the
1024 px photo while the page still showed the 96 px one). Sam chose to
fix it: `uploadProfileImage` now saves the address with `?v=<ms>` —
the file is still one per student, overwritten in place, but every
upload is a new address (a small mechanism change, Sam's ruling). A
second fix commit. Proven in the pane: a red 200 px PNG → Save → the
page and the sidebar showed it at once, both on the stamped address;
the "After the rebuild" line is marked done. The two URL checks
(`isSafeAvatar`, `isSafeUrl`) accept the query string.

### Found: dev's quizzes pointed at legacy question ids (dev data, fixed by hand)

Sam's first Start opened a runner with no questions. Cause: 5a copied
the five fixed quizzes and two mock exams from the legacy tables
(§6.6), so their `item_ids` carry legacy ids (`GP_001` …), while 4a
loaded dev's bank from Sam's CSV exports (`GP-S1-78` …); no quiz id
existed in dev's bank. Not a code defect and not a prod risk — at
cutover the §6.6 SQL copies the bank and the quizzes from the same
source, so they match. **Fixed on dev by hand** (MCP `execute_sql`,
seven single-row updates, no migration): each quiz now lists the first
N ids of its course's dev items table, N unchanged; verified every id
resolves (10 / 10 × 5 fixed, 25 / 25 and 20 / 20 mock). Sam's stale
RN_MED attempt from before the fix was marked abandoned. A quiz created
on dev through the admin picker picks dev ids and needs nothing.

### Learned

- The dev MCP's classifier refuses a `DO $$ … EXECUTE format(…) $$`
  block (read as a mass delete); the same work as plain single-row
  `UPDATE … RETURNING` statements passes.
- The React compiler lint rule forbids `setState` inside an effect; a
  "mounted" flag is `useSyncExternalStore(subscribeNever, () => true,
  () => false)`.
- At session start the pane holds no session; a student-side walk by
  Claude needs Sam to sign the pane in first (a password is Sam's to
  type). Plan the walk so Sam signs in early or tests directly.

## Session — 2026-09-14 — **6a passed Sam's test; 6b built by Codex (the timed mode on the shared core), tested by Sam, wrapped and merged by Claude** — Claude, for Codex's branch

Codex built 6b on `codex/slice-6b-timed-runner` (two commits, author
line Sam's git identity, 2026-09-14) and left the branch unpushed and
without a log entry or index lines. Claude found it at session start
(`git branch --no-merged main`), read every line of the diff, pushed
it as it was so nothing could be lost, and — after Sam's test — wrapped
it on `claude/slice-6b-wrap` (Codex's two commits, then the script
removal below, then this docs commit). `npm run lint:check` on the
branch: 0 known, 0 warnings, unchanged; `tsc --noEmit` clean. **The
merge to `main` was asked for at the end and is recorded by git, not
here.**

### 6a — Sam's test passed (2026-09-14)

Codex's first commit ticked 6a in `BUILD_LIST.md` and §14 after Sam's
walk (build an instant quiz, answer, close the tab, resume, finish,
review). Its tick carried the date 2026-09-13; corrected to the test's
date, 2026-09-14, in this docs commit.

### 6b — built by Codex, tested by Sam (2026-09-14)

**What Codex built** (`app/(app)/runner/timed/page.tsx`,
`components/runner/quiz-runner.tsx`, `lib/attempts/actions.ts`,
`lib/attempts/types.ts`, `styles/runner.css`): the exam page is the
instant page with `mode="timed"`; the core gained the timer bar (mm:ss,
the thin progress line, amber under 20 %, red and pulsing under 10 %),
the exam preflight (the warning box, "⏱ N minutes", "🎯 Start Exam" /
"🎯 Resume Exam", the resume test being "has a start stamp", not "has
answers"), feedback controls hidden until submit, the "⏰ Time is up!"
banner, auto-submit at zero, the timed words. Two Server Actions:
`markTimedAttemptStarted` — one conditional update (`time_taken_s is
null`) then a read-back, so two tabs opened together share one start
and a repeat call returns the same stamp; `saveTimedAttemptProgress` —
answers plus the elapsed time computed on the server from the stamp,
capped at the exam length, refused once the exam is no longer in
progress, and a no-op before the exam is started (a save from the
preflight cannot start the clock). `finishAttempt` now takes a timed
attempt's elapsed time from the server (the browser's number is for
instant only), and refuses a second submit or a stranger's. The
countdown recalculates from the stamped start on every tick and on
tab focus / visibility, never counting down a local number — a
backgrounded tab cannot gain time (legacy's setInterval would have
caught up on its next tick; same result, one step safer).

**Claude's review before the merge.** The design matches what was
agreed on 2026-09-13: one core, two pages, the server owning the
numbers. Three small things, none blocking: the no-op save before
start (legacy would have written and thereby started the clock, but
legacy could never reach that path — invisible); the two helper
scripts (below); the 6a tick date (above).

**The two scripts, removed** (Sam, 2026-09-14): Codex added
`scripts/preview-timed-runner.mjs` (an esbuild fixture rendering the
runner alone on port 3106 with fake rows, no login) and
`scripts/verify-timed-attempts.mjs` (a dev-only integration check of
the timed actions with a stubbed gate and disposable rows). Neither is
needed to run the product; `scripts/` is the lint baseline and the
migration runner (AGENTS.md) and the repo has no test convention.
Removed in `claude/slice-6b-wrap`; they stay in history on Codex's
branch. What the check proved, for the record: a save from the
preflight does not start the exam; two concurrent starts share one
stamp and a repeat start returns it; an instant attempt cannot be
started as timed; another user's start and save are refused; a timed
save stores the server's elapsed seconds and the flags; finish
recomputes tampered correctness (browser said wrong, server said
right → 100 %) and ignores the browser's elapsed time; a save or a
start or a second finish after completion is refused; a finish past
the deadline stores the capped time; an instant finish keeps its
browser-side time. All rows deleted afterwards.

**Sam's test** (2026-09-14, on `localhost:3000` served from the main
checkout on Codex's branch, as student1): a timed build → the exam
preflight → the countdown → the tab closed and reopened with the clock
still running → zero → auto-submit, "Time is up", the feedback shown.
Passed; 6b ticked.

### Found in the data, not the code (Sam decides)

- **Programme trials are 60 days on dev, not 7.** Every `*_TRIAL`
  product row carries `duration_days = 60` (copied from the live
  catalogue in slice 3); only `WELCOME_TRIAL` is 7 and no programme
  points at it. The code takes the product's duration, as legacy did;
  the plan's "7-day trial" came from doc 02. A product edit if Sam wants
  7; not a code change.
- On dev the trial's expiry lands an hour later in the day than its
  start: the trial adds days in the dev machine's local time and the
  October clock change falls inside the 60 days (legacy did the same in
  the browser). Cloudflare runs in UTC, so not on prod.

### Found in legacy, carried as is (logged, not fixed)

  will extend an existing row; the real expiry is further out. Legacy.
- An expiry saved as 23:59:59.999 UTC shows as the next day's date in a
  browser ahead of UTC (the pane showed "21 Sept" for the 20th). Ghana
  is UTC; legacy did the same.
- The Edit dialog's date inputs take the row's first ten characters, so
  an expiry edited once becomes end-of-day; a start becomes midnight.
  Legacy.
- The `users` grant from the admin Users page (legacy
  `assignSubscription`, a direct insert, no extend) is slice 14's.

### Learned

  transaction**, so `begin; set local role authenticated; select
  set_config('request.jwt.claims', …, true); … rollback;` tests a policy
  as a given user without a browser session.

### One legacy defect not carried (not in §9 — logged, Sam decides)

The fixed-quiz admin list selected only the list's columns (legacy
`getAllQuizzesPaginated`: no `item_ids`, `time_limit_sec`, `shuffle`,
`notes`, dates), then opened a quiz for edit from that list. So on a
fresh page load, Edit came up with **no questions pre-selected, no time
limit, no schedule and no notes**; an admin had to re-pick every
question to get past the picker, and saving wiped the time limit,
schedule and notes. The mock page loaded whole rows and had no such
gap. The edit step now reads the full row (legacy's own `getQuizById`)
for both pages. Reaches an admin only, but a save from that form
changed what students got. **Built as the fix, flagged here; one call
in `openEdit()` to revert if Sam wants it as legacy.**

### Found in legacy, carried as is (not in §9; logged, not fixed)

- Whether the picker reloads on "Next" was a heuristic in legacy (the
  first loaded item's id prefix against the course id's prefix — RN_MED
  and RN_SURG share "RN"); here it reloads when the loaded course is not
  the selected one. Same outcome for every real case.
- After a save, legacy re-read the whole table (unpaginated) then went
  back to the list; here the fixed page re-reads its first page with the
  current search. Same screen.
- The mock Archive confirm says "mock exam", the fixed one "quiz"; both
  toggle archived ↔ active — a draft that is "restored" becomes active.
- Time limit `min=60` on the input is not enforced in the script; a
  smaller number saves. Legacy too.
- Dev's seven copied quizzes point at item ids from dev's old sample
  bank, which are not in the prod-exported `items_*`; on dev they open
  with 0 pre-selected. Prod's ids will match at cutover.

### Learned

- **`form_input` on a React checkbox does not reach React** — the box
  looks ticked, the state is not. Click the label. Text inputs and
  selects through `form_input` are fine.
- No `python` on Sam's machine; splicing scripts are node.

`lib/bank/csv.ts`: `readCsv` (quotes, `""`, newlines in quotes, CRLF,

(type upper-cased default MCQ, marks default 1, shuffle false only on

the rules again on what arrives, upsert on `item_id` 50 at a time, the
two counts, batch errors returned (legacy wrote them to the browser

### Verified in the browser (the pane signed in as Sam's admin)

4a: GP loads 601, dropdowns filled from data; Hard → 75, "kwashiorkor"
→ 5; edit GP-S0-35's subtopic → toast + pill (reverted by SQL); New
with a canvas-made PNG → row, bucket object, public URL 200 image/png,
panel flips to Edit with Delete; RPHN_DISEASE_CTRL → the empty state;

five-row file → "3 valid rows … 2 row(s) will be skipped" with the right
reasons; the comma-and-quote stem and the line-break stem stored intact;

self-closed; re-import with a changed stem → "1 imported", still 604.
Test rows deleted by SQL. No console errors anywhere.

### Found in legacy, carried as is (not in §9; logged, not fixed)

- Opening a question runs `onTypeChange()` after the fields are filled,
  so the shuffle box follows the TYPE, not the stored value; saving
  writes the type default back. Reaches an admin only.
- The message after any save is "Question saved." — the "created"
  branch runs after the flag is already cleared.
- The importer's message says "must have at least 2 options" but the
  check skips only when BOTH option_a and option_b are blank; one option
  passes.
- Deleting a question leaves its image in the bucket (legacy too).
- Below 1100 px the edit panel sits under the whole list (a long scroll
  on a phone). Legacy's layout; slice 15 decides.

### Learned

- **The Supabase Table Editor exports one page.** 100 rows by default,
  1,000 at most; the first export of every table was the first page.
  Check the count against the April inventory before loading. The
  1,080-row table needed two pages joined (no overlap, checked).
- **A file set by page script works in the pane**: a `File` placed on
  the input through `DataTransfer` plus a bubbling `change` event reaches
  React's handler. Native `confirm()` still does not.
- A heredoc of more than ~100 lines fails to parse in the Bash tool with
  no file written; one file per call, or the Write tool.

as legacy. `actions.ts` — the pages' direct inserts / updates / delete
as Server Actions behind `requireAdmin()`, repeating each page's

Products: the empty-submit toast; create `SLICE3_TEST` (uppercased id,
`GHS 12.50` → 1250 minor, 30 d, one course); the row, the panel, Edit
prefilled, the tag input adds on a real Enter; Archive modal with the

courses →" jump; create a draft course with two programmes (uppercased
id, "2 programmes selected"); panel shows programme names; Archive
(row dimmed) and Restore; rename a programme (id hidden on edit) — the
register dropdown showed the new name at once. Config: the bad-key
toast, Add, Save with "✓ Saved". Premium Prep: with `RN_2026_PREP`
restored, "Starting from GHS 99.00", the card, summary chips, step 2,

written, pre-select from `?program_id=RN` with the remembered email
and phone. Both public pages at 375 px with no sideways scroll.

**Not verified: Config → Delete.** It calls the browser's native
`confirm()`, which the pane's script tool cannot answer from inside
the page, and the pane's admin session was then kicked by the
two-device cap (Sam signed in twice on his side, so the older pane
session went, exactly as legacy does). ⬜ Sam's test. Creating a
throwaway account through the browser is something I do not do.

**dev data after the session**: my test rows (`SLICE3_TEST`,
`SLICE3_TEST_COURSE`, `slice3_test`) deleted by SQL on dev and the
programme name reverted — counts back to 34 / 11 / 7. Then, on Sam's
word at the very end: **every archived product on dev set back to
active (23 rows, 34 of 34 now active).** ⭐ Archiving WAS the legacy
pause: to stop sales, Sam archived every paid product, and only the
free ones stayed active — that is why the copy arrived with 24
archived. On dev they must be buyable for testing. Among the 23 are
two old test rows (`TEXT_NEW_PRODUCT`, `CCJHBC_IDUDKHID`) and
`WELCOME_TRIAL`; Sam decides next session whether those three go back.
Cutover re-copies from prod (§6.6), so nothing here carries — and the
prod copy will arrive paused, as prod is today, until Sam un-archives.

### Learned

- **The pane's script tool runs in an isolated world**: it sees the
  DOM, but `window.confirm = () => true` there does not reach the
  page's own `confirm`. A native dialog cannot be answered that way.
- **The pane's "Return" key does not reach an `onKeyDown` Enter
  handler**; a synthetic `KeyboardEvent('keydown', { key: 'Enter' })`
  from the script tool does. Test key handlers that way.
- **Two pages written in parallel can leave a stale "Module not found"
  in the console** when the server page is compiled before its client
  half exists; the next compile clears it. Not a defect.
- A long multi-file heredoc chain in one shell call fails to parse on
  this Windows shell; one file per write.

  three admin pages (Config → Delete in particular), the landing page,

gates; anything else → /router, which refuses it (§9 #12).

**Pages**: /login (three doors), /register (nine fields, the two
touchpoints, rollback per §9 #4), /forgot-password, /reset-password,
/router, /logout (POST from the button; GET only with `?via=gate`),
`(app)/layout.tsx`. Every string is the legacy page's; errors and
notices are toasts (UI convention #1) via `lib/toast/toast.tsx`.
`styles/base.css` (tokens, reset, toast) and `styles/auth.css`
(style.css auth block + the three pages' inline blocks).

⭐ **Two link shapes, two clients.** The magic link and the reset
email are sent through a plain IMPLICIT-flow client, not the cookie
client: the SSR client's PKCE flow ties a link to the browser that
asked, and a student who opens her email on her phone must still get
in. Those links return with tokens in the hash and the page calls
`setSession` itself; Google (started by the browser client) returns
`?code=` and the library exchanges it. /login and /reset-password
handle both shapes (the MyNclex reset page is the reference).

**Verified in the browser** (throwaway `slice2a-test@example.com`,
left on dev): register end to end and the profile row; wrong password
→ toast, right password → dashboard; LOGIN_FAIL + LOGIN_SUCCESS rows
with device label, ua/ip/fp hashes; three logins → oldest session
kicked, two live; a kicked device is signed out on its next page and
lands on /login with cookies cleared; sign out; ADMIN → admin
dashboard and through the student gate; STUDENT bounced off /admin;
the reset page's two dead ends; both pages at 375 px.

### 2b — shell

`lib/nav/{types,student,admin}.ts`: both menus as data in legacy

(active link, the three student dropdowns, My Courses from course

`components/shell/mobile/mobile-drawer.tsx` (ONE `<aside>` for every
width: fixed on desktop, slid in by ☰/✕ over a backdrop under 768 px;
closes on backdrop, link tap, Escape, route change — the shared
drawer of UI convention #3), the two thin audience sidebars,
`AppShell`, `PageHeader` (legacy `.top-nav`: title, subtitle, name,
Sign out as a POST form), `styles/shell.css`. Audience layouts run the
gate and wrap in the shell; `loadGate` is `cache()`d so layout + page
cost one lookup. The dashboards carry the legacy headers ("Welcome
back, {forename}!") and a one-line card until slices 7 and 14.

**No footer**: no legacy page has one; the 2b line said "footer" from
MyNclex habit. **Dropped rows**: "Teacher Assess" (MyTeacher's).
**Hidden row**: "✈️ Telegram" — see below. Verified: every item
renders, dropdowns open/close, "No courses found", the admin drawer on
a phone with all 13 rows, backdrop and Escape close it.

### Telegram is not dead (Sam) — slice 17

§9 #10 called the Telegram sidebar item dead. Wrong: git history
holds a Sheets-era **TelegramGate** — a Connect Telegram page
(commit 737237c, 712 lines) issuing a 10-minute `QK-` link code, and
the bot's Apps Script (`Telegram_By_Worker-DEV`, 1,322 lines) checking
it against five sheet tables (groups, allowlist, link_codes,
telegram_links, audit) and admitting students to their product's
groups; the bot Worker still sits on the workspace CF account. The
first rebuild moved none of it; `products.telegram_group_keys`, the
admin field and the portal-guide text are the survivors. Queued as
**slice 17** after 8; §9 #10 corrected; the row stays in
`lib/nav/student.ts` with `hidden: true` until its page exists.

### Dev accounts for Sam (hand step on dev, not a migration)

The login table is project-wide, so a legacy profile row copied into
`licensure_gh.users` signs in with its existing password. Copied by
one guarded `insert … select from public.users` on dev (MCP
`execute_sql`; the schema is ours, so no tracker is touched):
`mybackpacc@gmail.com` (ADMIN), `mybackpacc+mynmclicensurestudent1`
(STUDENT, RN) and `…student2` (STUDENT, RM), same `U_` ids as legacy,
no school, no subscriptions (no table yet). The three deactivated
`+test1..3` accounts were left. **Dev only** — prod gets no user rows
(D5, D7).

### Learned (→ AGENTS.md workarounds)

- A `<form action={fn}>` RESETS its fields after the action returns
  (React 19). The register form's first submit only opens a confirm
  modal, and "Go back" must find the fields intact — so that submit is
  a plain `onSubmit` + `new FormData(form)`.
- A Server Component cannot clear cookies; a gate that must sign
  someone out redirects to `/logout?via=gate` (a Route Handler).
- The middleware's "signed-in user leaves /login" bounce must be
  GET-only: a Server Action posted to /login after a Google return
  arrives WITH a user and must reach the action.
- A desktop-app worktree has no `node_modules` (`npm ci` there) and
  gets a COPY of `.env.local` at creation; a key added to the main
  file later must be copied across by hand.
- The Browser pane's clicks time out under phone emulation; drive the
  element from `javascript_tool` and read the state back.

### Open

- ⬜ Sam's tests, one real email each: forgot → reset link; magic link
  (an address with an account, and one without); Continue with Google.
  If Google refuses the return, `http://localhost:3000/login` must be
  in the Supabase redirect list exactly.
- ⬜ Registration grants no trial and sends no welcome email yet
  (slices 8, 10); the success screen still says "We've also sent a
  welcome email", legacy text.
- ⬜ The register page's trial hint ("You'll get a 7-day free trial")

- ⬜ The magic link creates an auth user for an unknown address

- ⬜ Prod Worker: `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` (prod project's) before

  sender domain; MyNclex's dev Worker still on the personal CF account.

## Session — 2026-09-10 (evening) — **the release branch is `production`; one Cloudflare account queued as 1c** — Claude

**No code built, no dev server started (Sam's ask).** Two small
commits on `claude/work-session-setup-e48575`, then the docs commit;
the merge to `main` was asked for at the end and is recorded by git,
not here. Session started from the previous entry: `main` at the
slice 1 tick, nothing unmerged, port 3000 free (the server left running
last session was gone).

### Found at load: there is no `prod` branch

The remote has `origin/production` — the gamma-era release branch, 18
commits behind `main`, carrying the vanilla site's release merges
("Release main → production — …"). The two prod workflows listened for
a push to `prod`, so a release push would have gone nowhere. ⭐ Sam's
call: **keep the name `production`, change the repo.** Reasons: two
lines in two workflow files versus renaming a branch on GitHub (any
old Pages hookup, other clones, release commits that name it); the
sibling repos' release history uses `production` too. The old commits
on it are history, kept; the first release merge of `main` into it
carries the rebuild over them, which is the normal path anyway. Changed:
`deploy-prod.yml`, `migrate-prod.yml` (the branch line), `AGENTS.md`
(the branching section, which now also says what the older commits
are), `README.md`, `SESSIONS.md`, `db/README.md`, `rebuild.md` §3.1
and §6.5. Everything named "prod" that is an environment label —
Worker names, `env.prod`, the DB secrets — is untouched.

### Sam's question 1 — one Cloudflare account

Context: dev Workers sit on the personal CF account, prod Workers on
the workspace account (qacademynurses), and the paid plan is now
charged on both. Sam wants one account. Answer, worth keeping:

- A Worker is a named thing inside an account; `licensure-dev` and
  `licensure-prod` sit side by side with no conflict. The plan fee is
  per account, so one account is one fee. The split was history, not a
  need.
- ⭐ **Prod must be on the workspace account**, because a Worker can
  take a custom hostname only when the zone (`quademia.com`) is in the
  same account. Dev has no domain tie, so dev is what moves. Once every
  dev Worker (MyNclex's too) is off the personal account, that account
  drops to free — that is where the saving is.
- For this product it is one `account_id` line at the top of
  `wrangler.jsonc`, the dev `APP_ORIGIN` following the new
  `workers.dev` address, and `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN_DEV` issued from the
  workspace account. Nothing is deployed yet, so do it once and never
  move. **Queued as 1c in `BUILD_LIST.md`, before 1b.** MyNclex moves
  at its own pace.

### Sam's question 2 — dev and prod as two schemas in one Supabase project

Rejected, and Sam dropped it. A schema separates *products* within an
environment; it cannot separate *environments*, because the dangerous
shared things are project-wide, not schema-scoped: **one `auth.users`
table** (a dev test signup would sit beside a paying student, with no
way apart), one anon + service-role key pair (a leaked dev key is the
prod key), one Storage, one set of auth/email/redirect settings, one
connection pool and resource limit, and backups/restores per project.
The right axis is one project per environment, one schema per product
— which is exactly gamma's pair as it stands, at no extra cost. The
only cheaper shape is local Supabase (Docker) for dev with prod alone
in the cloud, which would leave the deployed `licensure-dev` Worker
with no database; not taken.

### Noticed, not fixed

- `rebuild.md` §6.1 table still says the new product is `licensure.*`
  — left from before the D10 rename to `licensure_gh`. Doc-only, reaches
  nobody; flagged to Sam, not corrected this session.

`npm run lint:check`: "No new lint errors. 0 known errors, 0 warnings —
unchanged." Run from the main checkout, because the session worktree

reported "no lintable files staged" on every commit. ⓘ A desktop-app

Recorded as D1–D9 in `rebuild.md` §2 with the alternative and the
reason. The ones that changed Claude's first proposal:

  nclex are totally different… the only thing we are doing here is

`preview_start` launched the **MyNclex** app the first time — the
launcher resolves against the session's home folder, not the attached
one; the licensure server was then started from its own folder. This
is the concrete reason for *Session mode* below.

**Then verified, later the same evening:** Sam pasted the session-pooler
URI into `.env.local` and `npm run db:migrate` applied the first
migration to dev — checked from inside the database: schema present,
one tracker row, usage granted to the three API roles. Sam then added
`licensure_gh` to Exposed schemas + Extra search path; the API check
went from `PGRST106 Invalid schema` (listing `api, public,
graphql_public`) to `PGRST205 could not find the table`, which is the
right answer for an empty exposed schema, and the tracker is refused to
anon (42501) as intended. **Slice 1 ticked ✅ 2026-09-10**; the deploy
half is a ⬜ 1b line until the four GitHub secrets exist. Two small
things learned: the pasted password contains an `@` and the `postgres`
library parsed it anyway (no encoding needed, but `%40` is the safe
form if a future password breaks); the runner now silences Postgres
NOTICEs, because the first migration deliberately repeats the
bootstrap's `IF NOT EXISTS` statements. ⓘ The dev project also has an
`api` schema exposed that nobody in this session created — noted, not
touched.

⚠ **Security advisory, who it reaches:** `npm audit` flags the pinned
Next.js 16.2.4 (the same pin as MyNclex) as critical — a middleware
bypass via segment-prefetch routes, plus cache poisoning and a DoS,
fixed in a later 16.3 release. Reaches nobody here (nothing deployed);
reaches MyNclex's dev and prod Workers, which put auth middleware in
front of student and tutor pages. Not changed here — the stack rule is
to match MyNclex, and the OpenNext adapter may not take a newer Next.
**Open item for the MyNclex repo first, then mirrored here.**

### Part five — the schema is `licensure_gh`, decided before it existed

While explaining the dashboard step Sam asked whether the "folder"
should say Ghana, since Nigeria, Kenya or the UK could follow — and
then saw the other axis himself: *"licensure is not limited to nursing
or NMC… the same product could serve teacher licensure."* ⭐ Recorded
as **D10**: the schema names the country and not the profession,
because a second profession is a *programme* inside the product (its
own courses, banks, products; everything else shared) while a second
country is the axis that does not fit inside the data. "NMC" would not
even distinguish Ghana from the UK. The brand "MyNMCLicensure" is what
would change on the day a teacher programme arrives — *"it would be a
matter of rebranding"* — and a brand is cheap to change; a schema is
not. Renamed everywhere it was written (clients, middleware, runner,
migration file and body, workflows, `db/README.md`, AGENTS, README,
plan, index), the bucket prefix with it (`licensure-gh-…`); Worker
names, repo name and `licensure.quademia.com` deliberately untouched.
`tsc` clean after. ⚠ This was free only because no migration had run;
the log records that the window was used.

### Session mode (Sam asked; answer worth keeping)

Claude Code's "project" is the folder a session is opened in: that
folder's `CLAUDE.md` is auto-loaded, its memory directory is separate,
its history is separate. **One session per repo, opened in that repo's
folder.** Attaching another repo's folder to a session is right for
cross-repo discussion (today's morning) and wrong for building (today's
afternoon — the launcher symptom above). Codex has the same shape via
`AGENTS.md`. The memory Claude wrote today lives under the MyNclex
project and points here; a fresh licensure session starts from this
log, which is the design.
